```
691	export async function patchDraft(iCalUid: string, patch: DraftPatch | undefined): Promise<VisitDraft | null> {
692	  const draft = await getDraft(iCalUid);
693	  if (!draft) return null;
694	  if (patch?.location !== undefined) draft.location = patch.location;
695	  for (const gp of patch?.guests ?? []) {
696	    const guest = draft.roster.find((g) => g.email.toLowerCase() === gp.email.toLowerCase());
697	    if (!guest) continue;
698	    if (gp.include !== undefined) guest.include = gp.include;
699	    if (gp.name !== undefined) guest.name = gp.name;
700	    if (gp.phone !== undefined) guest.phone = gp.phone;
701	    if (gp.passTemplateKey !== undefined) guest.passTemplateKey = gp.passTemplateKey;
702	    if (gp.emailTemplateKey !== undefined) guest.emailTemplateKey = gp.emailTemplateKey;
703	  }
704	  await upsertDraft(draft);
705	  return draft;
706	}
707	
708	/** Record created invites back onto the draft (status + invitationId) and optionally new times. */
709	export async function markMaterialized(
710	  iCalUid: string,
711	  passes: { visitorEmail: string; invitationId: string }[],
712	  times?: { eventStart?: string; eventEnd?: string },
713	): Promise<void> {
714	  const draft = await getDraft(iCalUid);
715	  if (!draft) return;
716	  for (const pass of passes) {
717	    const guest = draft.roster.find((g) => g.email.toLowerCase() === pass.visitorEmail.toLowerCase());
718	    if (guest) {
719	      guest.invitationId = pass.invitationId;
720	      guest.status = 'sent';
721	    }
722	  }
723	  draft.materialized = true;
724	  if (times?.eventStart !== undefined) draft.eventStart = times.eventStart;
725	  if (times?.eventEnd !== undefined) draft.eventEnd = times.eventEnd;
726	  await upsertDraft(draft);
727	}
728	
729	// ─── Materialize ──────────────────────────────────────────────
730	
731	/**
732	 * Create one visitor invite per INCLUDED guest, honoring the host's overrides.
733	 * Reuses `createVisitorInvite` (the shared cross-channel entry) — no logic fork.
734	 * Caller is responsible for cancelling any prior invites and writing the
735	 * `calendar_event_links` ledger row (so dedup/reschedule converge).
736	 */
737	export async function materializeDraft(
738	  draft: VisitDraft,
739	  opts: { hostName?: string; rescheduled?: boolean } = {},
740	): Promise<{ invitationIds: string[]; passes: PassResult[] }> {
741	  // Pass duration must equal the event window — never guess (decision 2026-06-17).
742	  const window = resolveEventWindow(draft);
743	  if (!window) {
744	    console.warn(`[VisitDraft] materialize skipped for ${draft.iCalUid}: ${EVENT_TIME_MISSING}`);
745	    return { invitationIds: [], passes: [] };
746	  }
747	  const { start, end } = window;
748	
749	  const resolvedHost = draft.hostEmail
750	    ? await resolveHostContactByEmail(draft.hostEmail).catch(() => null)
751	    : null;
752	  // Prefer the Workspace-resolved name; only fall back to opts.hostName when it's
753	  // a real name (not the email the ID token gives us). Never show the email.
754	  const hostName = await resolveHostDisplayName(draft.hostEmail, opts.hostName);
755	  // On a reschedule, the guest already had a pass → frame the new one as updated.
756	  const emailOverride = opts.rescheduled ? { ...RESCHEDULED_EMAIL_OVERRIDE } : undefined;
757	
758	  const passes: PassResult[] = [];
759	  for (const guest of draft.roster) {
760	    if (!guest.include || !guest.email) continue;
761	    const visitorName = guest.name?.trim() || nameFromEmail(guest.email);
762	    try {
763	      const result = await createVisitorInvite({
764	        visitorName,
765	        visitorEmail: guest.email,
766	        visitorPhone: guest.phone || undefined,
767	        purpose: draft.title,
768	        hostEmail: draft.hostEmail,
769	        hostEmployeeId: resolvedHost?.staff?.id,
770	        hostEmployeeName: hostName ?? draft.hostEmail,
771	        expectedDate: istDate(start),
772	        expectedTime: istTime(start),
773	        validFrom: start.toISOString(),
774	        validUntil: end.toISOString(),
775	        emailTemplateKey: guest.emailTemplateKey,
776	        emailTemplateOverride: emailOverride,
777	        passTemplateKey: guest.passTemplateKey,
778	        invitedVia: 'calendar',
779	        invitedBy: draft.hostEmail,
780	        batchId: draft.iCalUid,
```

```
499	export async function getOrCreateDraft(
500	  input: BuildDraftInput,
501	  opts: GetOrCreateOpts = {},
502	): Promise<VisitDraft> {
503	  const built = await buildDraft(input);
504	  let existing = await getDraft(input.iCalUid);
505	
506	  // Key-drift recovery: if not found by iCalUid but we have the stable Google
507	  // event id, find the draft under its old key and re-key it to the now-known
508	  // iCalUid. Prevents a flaky Events.get from rebuilding a fresh draft (which would
509	  // wipe the host's toggle/edit state).
510	  if (!existing && input.providerEventId) {
511	    const byProvider = await getDraftByProviderEventId(input.providerEventId);
512	    if (byProvider && byProvider.iCalUid !== input.iCalUid) {
513	      // Move BOTH the draft and its ledger row onto the now-resolved iCalUid so
514	      // every later lookup (send, poll reschedule, cancel) stays consistent.
515	      await rekeyDraft(byProvider.iCalUid, input.iCalUid);
516	      await rekeyCalendarLink(byProvider.iCalUid, input.iCalUid).catch((e) => console.warn('[VisitDraft] link rekey failed:', e));
517	      existing = await getDraft(input.iCalUid);
518	    } else if (byProvider) {
519	      existing = byProvider;
520	    }
521	  }
522	
523	  // Link-only divergence (F1): a ledger row may exist for THIS occurrence under a
524	  // different iCalUid (e.g. the Slack-self-host D8 link, which writes no draft, or a
525	  // surface whose iCalUid drifted) while sharing the provider event id. Re-key it onto
526	  // the resolved iCalUid so the send/reschedule/cancel paths converge. `built`'s
527	  // adoption already resolved the pass for THIS open via the provider fallback; this
528	  // just keeps the ledger keyed consistently going forward.
529	  if (input.providerEventId) {
530	    const linkByProvider = await getCalendarLinkByProviderEventId(input.providerEventId).catch(() => null);
531	    if (linkByProvider && linkByProvider.ical_uid !== input.iCalUid) {
532	      await rekeyCalendarLink(linkByProvider.ical_uid, input.iCalUid)
533	        .catch((e) => console.warn('[VisitDraft] link rekey (provider) failed:', e));
534	    }
535	  }
536	
537	  if (!existing) {
538	    await upsertDraft(built);
539	    return built;
540	  }
541	
542	  const prevByEmail: Record<string, DraftGuest> = {};
543	  for (const g of existing.roster) prevByEmail[g.email.toLowerCase()] = g;
544	  const incoming = new Set(built.roster.map((g) => g.email.toLowerCase()));
545	
546	  // Safety: an EMPTY incoming list means "unknown" (a transient/partial payload),
547	  // not "every guest was removed" — never mass-cancel on it. Only reconcile
548	  // removals when we actually received attendees.
549	  const reconcileRemovals = !!opts.reconcileRemovals && built.roster.length > 0;
550	
551	  // 1) Current attendees first (new ones added; existing ones keep their edits).
552	  const roster: DraftGuest[] = built.roster.map((g) => {
553	    const prev = prevByEmail[g.email.toLowerCase()];
554	    if (!prev) return g;
555	    const name = prev.name && prev.name.toLowerCase() !== prev.email.toLowerCase() ? prev.name : g.name;
556	    // Re-derive `internal` from the fresh classification each open (cheap, and keeps
557	    // drafts saved before this flag existed correctly badged) while preserving the
558	    // host's edits (toggle/name/phone/status).
559	    return { ...prev, name, internal: g.internal };
560	  });
561	
562	  // 2) Guests in the draft but no longer on the event.
563	  for (const prev of existing.roster) {
564	    if (incoming.has(prev.email.toLowerCase())) continue;
565	    if (reconcileRemovals) {
566	      // Trusted card path: cancel a sent pass, then drop the guest from the list.
567	      if (prev.status === 'sent' && prev.invitationId) {
568	        console.log(`[VisitDraft] guest ${prev.email} removed from event → cancelling pass ${prev.invitationId}`);
569	        const ok = await cancelInvitationById(prev.invitationId).catch((e) => {
570	          console.warn(`[VisitDraft] cancel-on-remove ${prev.invitationId} failed:`, e);
571	          return false;
572	        });
573	        if (ok) await notifyInviteCancelled(prev.invitationId, { reason: 'You were removed from the calendar event.' });
574	      } else {
575	        console.log(`[VisitDraft] guest ${prev.email} removed from event (no active pass to cancel)`);
576	      }
577	      // dropped (not pushed)
578	    } else {
579	      // Conservative poll path: keep the guest untouched.
580	      roster.push(prev);
581	    }
582	  }
583	
584	  // 3) Self-heal: a stored 'sent' guest must be backed by a REAL pass. The load path
585	  // otherwise trusts the persisted status blindly, so a draft ever written 'sent'
586	  // (e.g. a wrongly-adopted, or since-cancelled/deleted, pass) shows "Pass sent"
587	  // forever even though no real pass backs it. Drop the claim when:
588	  //   - the invitation is gone/inactive (any guest), OR
589	  //   - the guest is an internal colleague (default-off) whose backing pass was NOT
590	  //     created via the calendar. The only legit way an internal colleague is 'sent'
591	  //     is the host toggling them on + send, which creates an invited_via='calendar'
592	  //     pass. A 'sent' internal guest backed by a pass from any OTHER channel
593	  //     (web/slack/excel/walk-in) is a phantom adoption of an unrelated same-day pass
594	  //     (the pre-fix bug — and the draft route then even linked it to this event, so
595	  //     a link/active check alone can't catch it).
596	  // Reset to the fresh classifier default so the card reflects reality.
597	  const builtByEmail: Record<string, DraftGuest> = {};
598	  for (const g of built.roster) builtByEmail[g.email.toLowerCase()] = g;
599	  for (const g of roster) {
600	    if (g.status !== 'sent' || !g.invitationId) continue;
601	    const active = await getActiveInvitationById(g.invitationId).catch(() => null);
602	    if (!isPhantomSentGuest(g, active)) continue;
603	    console.log(`[VisitDraft] guest ${g.email} stored 'sent' but no real pass backs it (active=${!!active}, internal=${!!g.internal}, via=${active?.invited_via ?? 'n/a'}) → resetting to pending`);
604	    // Also drop this event's stale link to the phantom pass (the first buggy open
605	    // linked it here as if we owned it) so a later move/delete can't reschedule or
606	    // cancel the colleague's unrelated pass.
607	    const phantomId = g.invitationId;
608	    await unlinkInvitationFromCalendar(phantomId).catch((e) => console.warn('[VisitDraft] unlink phantom failed:', e));
```

> AGENT

I've now traced all four issues through the code (content script, side panel, background sync, and the engine's draft persistence). Here's the analysis. **No code changed yet** — solutions are proposed for you to pick from.

---

## Issue 1 — Nudge shows "Visitor event needs passes." with no event name

**Root cause (high confidence):** that nudge is the **optimistic DOM guess**, not the sync. Two code facts:

- The sync path always sets a non-empty title: `title: ev.summary || '(no title)'` (`calendar-sync.ts:190`). So a sync-sourced nudge can never be *blank* — worst case it reads "(no title)".
- The optimistic path takes whatever the DOM scrape returned: `title: pendingMagic.title` where `pendingMagic.title = lastSnapshot.title ?? ''` (`calendar.content.ts:251-263`). When `snapshotOf` can't find the title node (`calendar.content.ts:227-238`), it's `''`, and the banner renders just the bold prefix with an empty slot (`calendar.content.ts:1000`).

So you saw the **instant** banner fire before the API reconciled it, and the DOM title scrape missed for that surface (the popover heading selector is brittle; on a freshly-created event the title input can be momentarily empty).

**It should self-heal** when the sync target (with the real title) arrives, because `merged()` lets the synced entry win by `eventId` (`calendar.content.ts:982`) — **but only if the optimistic eventId equals the sync eventId** (see Issue 4). If they differ, the titleless entry never gets replaced.

**Proposed solutions (pick):**
- **1A.** Don't emit an optimistic nudge at all when the title is empty — wait the ~3–8s for the sync burst (which always has a title). Simplest, removes the ugly state entirely; slight delay on the instant banner for title-less scrapes only.
- **1B.** Keep the instant nudge but render a complete fallback sentence ("A visitor event needs passes") with no dangling empty slot, then upgrade the text in place when the sync title arrives.
- **1C.** Harden `snapshotOf` title scraping further (more selectors). Lower value — brittle, and doesn't help if the scrape still misses.

I'd do **1A + 1B together** (don't show blank; if shown, show a complete sentence).

---

## Issue 2 — Editing a sent guest: button won't activate, then activates ~1s and reverts

This is **three distinct problems stacked**, which is why it feels totally broken:

**2a. Button stays disabled *while typing* (definite UX gap).** For a sent/materialized event, `canSend = hasChanges` (`VisitPanel.tsx:262`), and `hasChanges` is computed from the **draft/roster state + baseline diff** (`VisitPanel.tsx:217-224`). The TextField holds your keystrokes in **local component state** (`RosterRow.tsx:28`) and only commits to the draft on **blur** (`RosterRow.tsx:157,165`). So until you blur, the draft hasn't changed → the button is correctly-but-confusingly disabled.

**2b. On blur it activates ~1s then reverts (the real bug).** The blur fires `patch.mutate`. `onMutate` applies an optimistic update (`hooks.ts:176-181`) → button active. Then if the patch **fails**, `onError` rolls back to `ctx.prev` (`hooks.ts:182`) → the edit reverts and the button deactivates. The engine's `patchDraft` (`visit-drafts.ts:691-706`) *does* persist name/phone correctly and reload *preserves* edits (`visit-drafts.ts:555`), so **the revert is client-side rollback, not the server discarding the edit.**

**The most likely failure trigger is your Issue-#1-style token expiry.** `DRAFT_PATCH` → `withIdToken` → `getValidTokens()` (`background.ts:241`, `auth.ts:107`). If the cached access token has expired and the silent `prompt=none` renew fails, the RPC returns `needsAuth` and the patch rejects → silent rollback. Crucially, **the panel only routes `resolve`/`draft` auth errors to the sign-in gate** (`VisitPanel.tsx:146`) — **patch and send auth failures are never surfaced**; they just roll back quietly. Meanwhile the event view itself stays up because `resolve`/`draft` are served from cache (`staleTime`). That's exactly your symptom: event loads fine, edit silently reverts, no sign-in prompt.

A secondary possible contributor is a **post-save sync refetch race**: `doSync` broadcasts `REFRESH_ACTIVE` when the active event is in `changedIds` (`background.ts:141-143`), which invalidates `['draft']`; if that `DRAFT_LOAD` reads the engine *before* the in-flight PATCH commits, it returns the pre-edit roster. This is mostly a concern in the seconds right after create/save, less so minutes later.

**Proposed solutions (pick):**
- **2A (fix 2a).** Make the button reflect in-progress edits: either treat the TextField's live local value as a pending change, or commit on each change (debounced) instead of only on blur. Removes the "I typed but nothing lit up" confusion.
- **2B (fix 2b — surface auth).** Route `patch`/`send` `needsAuth` failures to the same sign-in/reconnect gate `resolve`/`draft` use, and show patch errors inline instead of silently rolling back. This makes the failure visible and recoverable.
- **2C (fix 2b — harden renew).** Investigate/strengthen the silent `prompt=none` renew (`auth.ts:110`); on renew failure trigger the reconnect-lapse flow proactively so writes don't dead-end. **This is the actual fix for your recurring "token expiry" pain** — 2B only makes it visible.
- **2D (fix the race).** Suppress `REFRESH_ACTIVE`-driven `['draft']` invalidation while a patch/send mutation is in flight (or skip refetch when the active event is being edited).

I'd prioritize **2C + 2B** (kill the silent token failure and stop hiding it), then **2A** (typing UX), and **2D** only if logs show the race.

**To confirm 2b's trigger:** reproduce with DevTools open on the side panel and watch for `[auxilio] engine ←` status and a `needsAuth`/401 on the PATCH. If you see the patch never reaching the engine, it's the token path (2C/2B). If it reaches the engine and succeeds but a later `DRAFT_LOAD` overwrites it, it's the race (2D).

---

## Issue 3 — Sent visitor event list not showing

Two things, one intentional and one a real bug:

**Intentional:** in my v0.9.0 `EmptyState`, the "Passes sent" section only renders when `sent.length > 0` (I gate it `{sent.length > 0 && …}`). So with zero sent events, the section is hidden by design. **I think that's arguably the wrong call** and contributed to your confusion — I'd rather always show the section with an explicit empty line, so the surface is predictable.

**The real bug:** you *did* send, and it's still empty. `listForPanel` builds **only from the synced `marked` set** and tags an event "sent" via `handled[eventId]` / `engineHandled` (`calendar-sync.ts` new `listForPanel`). But:
- `markHandled` on send writes to `handled`, **not** to `marked` (`background.ts:351`). `marked` is populated **only by the background list-sync**.
- So if the list-sync never captured the event into `marked` (the stale-token / attendee-trim / window gap from `Planning-docs/2026-06-23_extension_visitor_event_sync_visibility_bug.md` §5), then after sending there's nothing for `listForPanel` to tag — **neither pending nor sent.** The panel could send passes fine (it uses an authoritative `events.get`, bypassing the list-sync), yet the event is invisible to the homescreen.
- There's also a latent **id-keying mismatch risk**: `handled` is keyed by the eventId the *panel* resolved (often the DOM `data-eventid`), while `marked` is keyed by the *API* `ev.id`. For a single event these match; for recurring/edge cases they don't, and the "sent" tag lookup misses.

**Proposed solutions (pick):**
- **3A (strongest).** When the panel resolves/sends an event, **seed it directly into `marked`** from the authoritative `events.get` we already did (real iCalUid, title, start, magic-address confirmed). This makes any event you've touched appear deterministically — no dependency on the flaky list-sync — and also gives the nudge a real title (helps Issue 1). Key `handled` and `marked` by the **same** API event id.
- **3B.** Add the `events.get` fallback in the sync for `sync MISS` candidates (the planning-doc open item) — fixes attendee-trim misses generally, but more code and only helps events the list returns at all.
- **3C.** Always render the "Passes sent" section with an explicit empty state (predictable surface). Cheap; do regardless.

I'd do **3A + 3C**. 3A is the high-leverage fix and dovetails with Issue 1.

---

## Issue 4 — Your question: DOM-instant nudge vs API-slow nudge — double nudge or resolved internally?

**It's designed to resolve internally, and it does *as long as the two sources agree on `eventId`.*** Concretely:

- Both sources merge into one map keyed by `eventId`, **sync wins** (`calendar.content.ts:979-986`), so a matching pair collapses to a single banner.
- When you interact ("Manage" or dismiss), the `eventId` is added to a session `dismissed` set persisted in `storage.session` (`calendar.content.ts:1015-1016, 1030-1033`). `visible()` filters dismissed (`calendar.content.ts:989`), so the **same** eventId won't nudge again this session — even when the slower sync entry arrives later.
- `setTargets` also calls `dropOptimistic(t.eventId)` for arriving targets (`calendar-sync`→`calendar.content.ts:1045`), and optimistic guesses self-expire after a 12s TTL (`calendar.content.ts:962`).

**So for a single, non-recurring event whose DOM `data-eventid` decodes to the same id the Calendar API returns: no double nudge — fully resolved internally.**

**The failure mode is an `eventId` mismatch between the two sources**, which happens for:
- **Recurring instances** — DOM instance id vs API `singleEvents=true` instance id can differ in format.
- Any drift between the DOM `data-eventid` encoding and the API `ev.id`.

When they mismatch: the titleless optimistic entry and the real sync entry are **two map keys** → they can show together ("+1 more") or in sequence, and **dismissing/handling one doesn't suppress the other** (different key). This same mismatch also undermines `markHandled` suppression (Issue 2/3) and the sent-tag lookup (Issue 3) — it's a **cross-cutting id-consistency concern**, not just a nudge concern.

**Proposed solutions (pick):**
- **4A (root fix).** Make `eventId` canonical everywhere: have the optimistic path use the **same** id derivation the sync/API uses (resolve the DOM eid to the API event id, or always seed from `events.get` per 3A). One id space → merge/dedup/handled/sent all line up.
- **4B (mitigation).** When the optimistic and a sync target share the same `iCalUid` (once known) or `seriesId`, treat them as the same nudge unit even if `eventId` differs. Helps recurring without a full id overhaul.
- **4C.** Accept the small pre-reconcile window but make optimistic entries strictly transient (shorter TTL, never persisted to the badge/sent list). Lowest effort, doesn't fix recurring double-nudge.

I'd do **4A**, ideally via the same **3A** seeding mechanism (one authoritative id source kills three birds: Issue 1 title, Issue 3 sent-list, Issue 4 dedup).

---

## How the fixes cluster

There's a **single high-leverage change** behind three of the four issues: **seed the tracked set + handled/dedup from the panel's authoritative `events.get` (3A/4A)**, keyed by one canonical event id. That gives the nudge a real title (1), makes touched events appear in the sent list deterministically (3), and aligns the optimistic/sync ids so there's no double-nudge (4). Issue 2 is mostly separate (auth/token + UX), best fixed by **2C+2B** plus the **2A** typing affordance.

**Tell me which to implement** — e.g. "do 3A/4A, 2C, 2B, and 1A; skip the rest," or any subset — and I'll build them. I'd recommend that exact set as the minimal package that resolves all four reports.

```
commit 629fae4c7a519568ee498b2099fcc029ca756e8c
Author: junaid-appointy <junaid@appointy.com>
Date:   Tue Jun 23 21:33:10 2026 +0530

    feat: implement reconnect banner for auth lapse and improve event handling
    
    - Added a reconnect banner that appears when the background reports a recoverable auth lapse, ensuring users are notified when nudging is paused.
    - Enhanced event handling logic to prevent missed notifications for recurring events and ensure proper marking of handled events.
    - Updated calendar sync logic to include a new handled events tracking mechanism, allowing for better management of issued passes.
    - Improved user interface text for clarity and consistency across various components.
    - Refactored code for better readability and maintainability, including the introduction of new utility functions and state management for user interactions.
    - Bumped version to 0.6.1 to reflect significant updates and improvements.

+      // A dialog that isn't an event surface — e.g. Google's "Send update emails
+      // to existing guests?" save confirmation — must NEVER host our button, and
+      // while one is open we suppress entirely so we don't inject onto it (or

+      if (dialog) {
+        // Sticky recognition: once a dialog node is accepted as an event
+        // surface, keep treating it as one until it actually leaves the DOM.
+        // Google re-renders the popover's innards constantly; isEventDialog is a
+        // heuristic, so a single tick where it momentarily reads false would
+        // otherwise yank the button and pop it back a frame later — the flicker.
+        // A genuinely different dialog (a confirmation) is a different node, so
+        // it's still evaluated strictly and correctly rejected.
+        const isEvent = dialog === eventDialog || isEventDialog(dialog);

+        eventDialog = dialog;
```

> AGENT

Confirmed — line `background.ts:57-61`: `onInstalled` → `clearSyncToken()` → full re-scan, and that listener fires on every install/update/**reload** of the unpacked extension.

I found the regression. It's **commit `629fae4` (2026-06-23, "implement reconnect banner… and improve event handling")** — the same commit that added the handled-tracking + "self-healing full re-scan." It introduced two coupled mechanisms that, together, *destroy* a known event's title instead of just refreshing it.

## The regression

`629fae4` made two changes that bite together:

1. **Full re-scan on every reload.** `chrome.runtime.onInstalled` now calls `clearSyncToken()` → the next sync is a **full, tokenless** scan (`background.ts:57-61`). This fires on every extension install/update/**reload** — which you do constantly while testing a new build.

2. **The full re-scan rebuilds `marked` from scratch.** In `doRunSync`: `const marked = fullSync ? {} : { ...prevMarked }` (`calendar-sync.ts:152`). On a full sync it starts **empty** and only re-adds events that *this one* `events.list` call returns with the magic address in attendees.

3. **That list call uses `timeMin = now`** (`calendar.ts:196`) — so it only ever sees events starting **in the future**. An all-day event for today, or one that already started, is **excluded**.

**Net effect:** every time you reload the extension, the known-events set is wiped and rebuilt from a single forward-only list call. Any visitor event that call doesn't return as marked — because it already started (timeMin), because Google's list API is lagging seconds after you created it, or because attendees got trimmed — is **dropped from `marked`**.

## Why this matches "it used to work"

**Before `629fae4`:** `marked` was carried forward incrementally and only rebuilt on a `410` token expiry. Once an event landed in `marked` (with its `summary`/title and start), it **stayed**. So the **named** nudge — which comes from the sync set via `listMarked` → `broadcastNudge` → `setTargets` — reliably arrived and replaced the instant optimistic guess. You saw the event name.

**After `629fae4`:** the reload-wipe + `timeMin=now` rebuild means the named sync target often never arrives. So the only thing left is the **optimistic DOM nudge**, whose title scrape can come back empty → **"Visitor event needs passes."** with no name. The name you "used to see" was the sync target landing; now it doesn't land.

## This is the single root cause behind three of your reports

All three flow from *"the event isn't in `marked`"*:

- **Titleless nudge (Issue 1):** named sync target never arrives → only the titleless optimistic shows.
- **Empty sent list (Issue 3):** `listForPanel` reads `marked` → nothing to tag as pending *or* sent.
- **"No upcoming visitor events" after refresh (your very first report):** `marked` got wiped on reload and the rebuild missed the event.

So my v0.9.0 "management surface" change couldn't help — it reads the same `marked` set that's being emptied.

## Fix options (pick later)

- **F1 — Merge, don't wipe (core correctness).** On a full sync, **union** the fresh scan with surviving `prevMarked` entries: keep a previously-marked event that's still in the forward window unless positively confirmed gone (a `cancelled` status, or an `events.get` says the magic address is gone). Stops the reload from destroying known events.
- **F2 — Widen the re-scan window.** Set `timeMin` to start-of-today (or `now − 24h`) instead of `now`, so today's in-progress / all-day visitor events are captured. Small, targeted.
- **F3 — Authoritative seed (also fixes title + dedup).** When the panel resolves/sends an event via `events.get` (authoritative attendees + real title + iCalUid), write it straight into `marked`. Anything you've touched is tracked regardless of the flaky list call. This is the 3A/4A idea from the earlier analysis — one change, three birds.
- **F4 — Don't `clearSyncToken` on every reload.** Only clear when the magic-address config actually changed (store + compare a hash). Removes the per-reload wipe while keeping the self-heal for genuine config changes. Cheapest, but doesn't fix the 12h periodic rebuild or the underlying list-miss.

My recommendation: **F1 + F2** as the correctness fix (stop the wipe, widen the window), plus **F3** if you want touched-events to be bulletproof. F4 alone only reduces frequency.

**Want empirical confirmation before fixing?** Open the **background** service-worker console, reload the extension, and watch for `[auxilio] sync done { mode: "full", itemsReturned, markedTotal }`. If `markedTotal` drops to 0 (or omits your event) right after the reload while the event clearly exists, that's the wipe confirmed.

Tell me which of F1–F4 (and which of the earlier 1A–4C) to implement, and I'll build them.

> AGENT

Magic address is `findajx@gmail.com`. Here's a copy-paste confirmation protocol — **no code changes**, run entirely from the background console. It will tell us *which* of the mechanisms is actually firing.

## Step 0 — Open the background console
`chrome://extensions` → enable Developer mode → find **Auxilio Visitor** → click **"Inspect views: service worker"**. That opens DevTools for the background worker (distinct from the side-panel and page consoles — make sure it's this one). Keep it open.

## Step 1 — Snapshot the current tracked set
Create your visitor event first, then paste this into the background console:

```js
chrome.storage.local.get([
  'auxilio.markedEvents','auxilio.syncToken','auxilio.handledEvents',
  'auxilio.engineHandled','auxilio.lastFullSync'
]).then(r => {
  const marked = r['auxilio.markedEvents'] || {};
  console.log('marked count:', Object.keys(marked).length);
  console.table(Object.values(marked).map(m => ({ title: m.title, start: m.start, eventId: m.eventId, iCalUid: m.iCalUid })));
  console.log('hasSyncToken?', !!r['auxilio.syncToken'],
              '| handled:', r['auxilio.handledEvents'],
              '| engineHandled:', r['auxilio.engineHandled'],
              '| lastFullSync:', r['auxilio.lastFullSync'] && new Date(r['auxilio.lastFullSync']).toLocaleString());
});
```

Note whether your event is in the table, and whether it shows under `handled` / `engineHandled`.

## Step 2 — The wipe test (the regression itself)
Reload the extension (`chrome://extensions` → the reload ↻ icon on the card). The worker restarts, so **reopen** "Inspect views: service worker". In the fresh console, look for:

```
[auxilio] sync done { mode: "full", magicAddress: "findajx@gmail.com", itemsReturned: …, markedTotal: …, newMarked: … }
```

(If nothing logs, the worker is idle — focus the Calendar tab or open the side panel to trigger a sync.) Then **re-run the Step 1 snippet**.

- **Event was in `marked` before the reload, gone after** → wipe-on-reload confirmed.
- Also watch for any `[auxilio] sync MISS: magic address in payload but isMarked() is false` — that's the attendee-trim signature.

## Step 3 — The smoking gun: what `events.list` actually returns
This calls the Calendar API exactly like the full re-scan does (`timeMin=now`), then again with a wider window, so we can see *why* the rebuild misses it:

```js
(async () => {
  const MAGIC = 'findajx@gmail.com';
  const r = await chrome.storage.session.get('auxilio.tokens');
  const tok = r['auxilio.tokens'];
  if (!tok) { console.warn('No cached token — open the side panel / sign in first, then retry.'); return; }
  const run = async (label, timeMin) => {
    const url = new URL('https://www.googleapis.com/calendar/v3/calendars/primary/events');
    url.searchParams.set('singleEvents','true');
    url.searchParams.set('maxResults','250');
    url.searchParams.set('timeMin', timeMin);
    url.searchParams.set('fields','items(id,iCalUID,summary,status,start,attendees(email,resource))');
    const res = await fetch(url, { headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${tok.accessToken}` } });
    const items = (await res.json()).items || [];
    console.log(`\n=== ${label} — ${items.length} events ===`);
    console.table(items.map(e => ({
      title: e.summary,
      start: e.start && (e.start.dateTime || e.start.date),
      hasMagic: (e.attendees||[]).some(a => (a.email||'').toLowerCase() === MAGIC),
      attendees: (e.attendees||[]).map(a=>a.email).join(', ')
    })));
  };
  const now = new Date();
  const startOfToday = new Date(now.getFullYear(), now.getMonth(), now.getDate()).toISOString();
  await run(`FULL RE-SCAN WINDOW (timeMin=now=${now.toISOString()})`, now.toISOString());
  await run(`WIDER WINDOW (timeMin=startOfToday=${startOfToday})`, startOfToday);
})();
```

## How to read it

| What you see | Conclusion | Fix it points to |
|---|---|---|
| Event present in **wider** window but **absent** in the `timeMin=now` window | `timeMin=now` excludes it (already started / all-day today) | **F2** (widen window) + **F1** (don't wipe) |
| Event present in both but **`hasMagic: false`** | Google trimmed the magic attendee out of `events.list` | **F3** (events.get fallback) |
| Event present in both with **`hasMagic: true`**, yet Step 2 showed it gone from `marked` after reload | Pure wipe/rebuild timing, or it's being dropped by `handled`/`engineHandled` (check Step 1) | **F1** (merge not wipe); if handled-flagged, the suppression logic |
| Event **absent from both** windows | `events.list` isn't returning it yet (consistency lag, secondary calendar, or not actually saved with the magic attendee) — wait ~30s and re-run Step 3 | not the wipe — a capture/lag issue |

Paste back the `[auxilio] sync done` line from Step 2 and the two tables from Step 3 (titles can be redacted — I mainly need `start`, `hasMagic`, and which window the event appears in), and I'll tell you definitively which fix to apply.

That output changes the diagnosis — and it **refutes my earlier hypothesis**. Good thing we checked before writing code. Let me be straight about what the data shows:

## My "wipe / timeMin" theory was wrong

- `markedTotal: 1`, and you saw the event in the Step 1 table. **The event IS being captured into `marked`, with its title.** So it's not being destructively wiped, and `timeMin=now` is not excluding it. F1/F2 are *not* the fix. Scratch them.

## What the data actually reveals

The smoking gun is `itemsReturned: 6041` and the Step 3 wall of **250 events all titled "Lunch"**. You have a **daily recurring "Lunch"** event with no end date. `events.list` is called with `singleEvents=true` and **no `timeMax`** (`calendar.ts:185-197`), so Google expands every recurring series into individual instances across the *entire* forward horizon (~2 years). One daily event alone = ~700 instances; your calendar expands to **6,041 items per full scan**.

That's the real problem, and here's how it produces your symptoms:

1. **A full re-scan must paginate all 6,041 items** (25 pages of 250) and only writes `marked` **after the whole loop finishes** (`calendar-sync.ts:236`). That takes many seconds.
2. **`629fae4` triggers a full re-scan on every extension reload** (`clearSyncToken` on `onInstalled`). During dev you reload constantly, so you repeatedly hit a multi-second window where the scan is mid-flight and `marked` is still stale/empty.
3. **MV3 kills idle service workers aggressively** (~30s, 5-min hard cap). A long 25-page fetch loop can be terminated before it writes `marked` — so that scan is abandoned with nothing saved, and the next reload starts over.

So during that window: the homescreen reads an empty/stale `marked` → **"No upcoming visitor events"**; the named nudge (which comes from `marked`) hasn't populated → only the **titleless optimistic DOM nudge** shows; the sent list is empty. Once a scan *does* complete (as one did — hence `markedTotal: 1`), it appears. That intermittency is exactly what you'd feel as "it used to work, now it doesn't" — and `629fae4` made it worse by running the expensive scan on every reload, not because of timeMin.

## The real fix direction

- **G1 — Bound the window with `timeMax`** (e.g. `now + 60–90 days`). Visitor nudges are near-term; this cuts 6,041 → a few hundred, so every scan is 1–2 pages and completes reliably before the worker is killed. **Highest impact.**
- **G2 — Stop full-rescanning on every reload.** Gate `clearSyncToken` on an actual magic-address config change (hash compare), so reloads don't keep re-triggering the expensive scan. (This is the old F4, still valid.)
- **G3 — Make the long scan resilient** (write `marked` progressively per page, or keep the worker alive). Lower priority once G1 shrinks the scan.

## One more empirical check (proves G1 before we build it)

Paste this in the background console — it paginates fully both ways and times them:

```js
(async () => {
  const MAGIC = 'findajx@gmail.com';
  const tok = (await chrome.storage.session.get('auxilio.tokens'))['auxilio.tokens'];
  if (!tok) { console.warn('sign in first'); return; }
  const fetchAll = async (timeMax) => {
    let pageToken, total = 0, pages = 0; const marked = [];
    do {
      const url = new URL('https://www.googleapis.com/calendar/v3/calendars/primary/events');
      url.searchParams.set('singleEvents','true'); url.searchParams.set('maxResults','250');
      url.searchParams.set('timeMin', new Date().toISOString());
      if (timeMax) url.searchParams.set('timeMax', timeMax);
      if (pageToken) url.searchParams.set('pageToken', pageToken);
      url.searchParams.set('fields','items(id,summary,start,attendees(email)),nextPageToken');
      const data = await (await fetch(url,{headers:{Authorization:`Bearer ${tok.accessToken}`}})).json();
      const items = data.items||[]; total += items.length; pages++;
      for (const e of items) if ((e.attendees||[]).some(a=>(a.email||'').toLowerCase()===MAGIC))
        marked.push({title:e.summary, start:e.start&&(e.start.dateTime||e.start.date)});
      pageToken = data.nextPageToken;
    } while (pageToken);
    return { total, pages, marked };
  };
  const plus90 = new Date(Date.now()+90*864e5).toISOString();
  console.time('unbounded'); const a = await fetchAll(null); console.timeEnd('unbounded');
  console.log('UNBOUNDED →', a.total, 'items,', a.pages, 'pages | visitor events:', a.marked);
  console.time('bounded90'); const b = await fetchAll(plus90); console.timeEnd('bounded90');
  console.log('timeMax=now+90d →', b.total, 'items,', b.pages, 'pages | visitor events:', b.marked);
})();
```

I expect the bounded call to drop from ~6,041/25 pages to a few hundred/1–2 pages and run several times faster, while still finding your visitor event. If it does, **G1 + G2** is the fix.

Two quick things to confirm so I know what you're even running:
1. **What version does `chrome://extensions` show for Auxilio?** My homescreen/"Passes sent" work is uncommitted (v0.9.0) and only loads after a `npm run build` + reload — if it shows **0.6.1**, the sent-list feature literally isn't in your build yet, which would independently explain "I don't see the sent list."
2. After a sync completes, does the event show on the homescreen **now**? That tells us whether anything downstream still filters it, or it was purely the slow-scan timing.

Definitive. The numbers settle it:

| Window | Items | Pages | Time |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Unbounded** (current behavior) | 6,034 | 25 | **14,298 ms** |
| **`timeMax = now+90d`** | 527 | 3 | **1,737 ms** |

Both still find your visitor event (`Meeting 20`, today). And you're on **0.9.0**, so my management-surface code *is* loaded — this was never a "feature missing" problem.

## Confirmed root cause

The full re-scan takes **~14 seconds** because `events.list(singleEvents=true)` with **no `timeMax`** expands your daily "Lunch" (and every other recurring series) into 6,000+ instances. Two things make that fatal:

- **MV3 kills the service worker** well before 14s of fetching reliably completes, and `marked` is only written *after* all 25 pages finish — so the scan often dies with nothing saved.
- **`629fae4` runs this 14s scan on every reload** (`clearSyncToken` on `onInstalled`), so during dev you constantly sit in the stale-`marked` window: homescreen empty, only the titleless optimistic nudge, empty sent list. When a scan *does* survive to completion, the event appears (your `markedTotal: 1`). That intermittency is the "it used to work" feeling.

Bounding to 90 days → **1.7s, 3 pages** — comfortably inside the worker's life. That's the fix.

## The fix I'd implement

- **G1 — Add `timeMax = now + 90 days`** to the tokenless `events.list` (`calendar.ts`). (`timeMin/timeMax` are incompatible with `syncToken`, and the code already sets `timeMin` only on the tokenless branch, so this is a clean one-line-family change. The existing 12h full re-scan slides the window forward.)
- **G2 — Stop full-rescanning on every reload.** Gate `clearSyncToken` on an actual config change: store a signature (`MAGIC_ADDRESS` + a `SYNC_SCHEMA_VERSION`), and on `onInstalled` only clear the token when it differs. Normal reloads then resume cheap incremental sync. Bumping `SYNC_SCHEMA_VERSION` now forces exactly **one** clean re-scan to establish the bounded window, then incremental forever after.

With G1+G2, `marked` populates in ~1.7s and survives reloads, which resolves the titleless nudge, "No upcoming visitor events," and the empty sent list together — all of them were downstream of the slow/fragile scan.

G3 (progressive/resilient writes) becomes unnecessary once the scan is 1.7s, so I'd skip it.

Two other things from the earlier analysis are **separate subsystems**, not fixed by G1+G2 — so I'm asking what scope you want:

Called on a real config change so it takes effect immediately\n *  instead of waiting for the periodic resync. */\nexport async function clearSyncToken(): Promise<void> {\n  await chrome.storage.local.remove([SYNC_TOKEN_KEY, LAST_FULL_KEY]);\n}\n\n/** Did the sync-relevant config (magic address or query schema) change since the\n *  last run? That's the ONLY time we must drop the token for a full re-scan. A\n *  plain reload leaves the signature unchanged, so it keeps the token and resumes\n *  cheap incremental sync — instead of re-running the multi-second full scan (which\n *  the MV3 worker can be killed in the middle of) on every reload. Records the new\n *  signature as a side effect. The 12h periodic full re-scan still self-heals drift. */\nexport async function syncConfigChanged(): Promise<boolean> {\n  const sig = `${MAGIC_ADDRESS}|v${SYNC_SCHEMA_VERSION}`;\n  const r = await chrome.storage.local.get(CONFIG_SIG_KEY);\n  if (r[CONFIG_SIG_KEY] === sig) return false;\n  await chrome.storage.local.set({ [CONFIG_SIG_KEY]: sig });\n  return true;\n}"
```json
}
```

Survives the restart so the hint is available. */\nconst EMAIL_KEY = 'auxilio.email';"

```json
  "old_string": "async function mint(interactive: boolean): Promise<{ bundle: TokenBundle; code?: string }> {\n  if (!OAUTH_CLIENT_ID) {\n    throw new Error(\n      'OAuth client id missing. Set WXT_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID in .env and rebuild.',\n    );\n  }\n  const url = new URL('https://accounts.google.com/o/oauth2/v2/auth');\n  url.searchParams.set('client_id', OAUTH_CLIENT_ID);\n  // Interactive: hybrid flow (+ offline) so we ALSO get a code to hand the engine\n  // for a calendar refresh token. Silent renew stays implicit (client tokens only).\n  url.searchParams.set('response_type', interactive ? 'code token id_token' : 'token id_token');\n  url.searchParams.set('redirect_uri', chrome.identity.getRedirectURL());\n  url.searchParams.set('scope', SCOPES);\n  url.searchParams.set('nonce', crypto.randomUUID());\n  url.searchParams.set('prompt', interactive ? 'consent select_account' : 'none');\n  if (interactive) url.searchParams.set('access_type', 'offline');\n\n  const redirect = await chrome.identity.launchWebAuthFlow({\n    url: url.toString(),\n    interactive,\n  });\n  if (!redirect) throw new Error('Sign-in was cancelled');\n\n  const { accessToken, idToken, code, expiresIn, error } = parseFragment(redirect);\n  if (error) throw new Error(`Google sign-in error: ${error}`);\n  if (!idToken || !accessToken) throw new Error('Sign-in did not return tokens');\n\n  const bundle: TokenBundle = {\n    idToken,\n    accessToken,\n    email: emailFromIdToken(idToken),\n    expiresAt: Date.now() + (expiresIn - 60) * 1000, // refresh 60s early\n  };\n  await chrome.storage.session.set({ [KEY]: bundle });\n  return { bundle, code: code || undefined };\n}",
  "new_string": "async function mint(\n  interactive: boolean,\n  loginHint?: string,\n): Promise<{ bundle: TokenBundle; code?: string }> {\n  if (!OAUTH_CLIENT_ID) {\n    throw new Error(\n      'OAuth client id missing. Set WXT_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID in .env and rebuild.',\n    );\n  }\n  const url = new URL('https://accounts.google.com/o/oauth2/v2/auth');\n  url.searchParams.set('client_id', OAUTH_CLIENT_ID);\n  // Interactive: hybrid flow (+ offline) so we ALSO get a code to hand the engine\n  // for a calendar refresh token. Silent renew stays implicit (client tokens only).\n  url.searchParams.set('response_type', interactive ? 'code token id_token' : 'token id_token');\n  url.searchParams.set('redirect_uri', chrome.identity.getRedirectURL());\n  url.searchParams.set('scope', SCOPES);\n  url.searchParams.set('nonce', crypto.randomUUID());\n  url.searchParams.set('prompt', interactive ? 'consent select_account' : 'none');\n  if (interactive) url.searchParams.set('access_type', 'offline');\n  // Tell Google which account to (re)use. Essential for the silent prompt=none\n  // renew on a multi-account profile — without it Google can't choose an account\n  // non-interactively and the renew fails, dead-ending every authed request.\n  if (loginHint) url.searchParams.set('login_hint', loginHint);\n\n  const redirect = await chrome.identity.launchWebAuthFlow({\n    url: url.toString(),\n    interactive,\n  });\n  if (!redirect) throw new Error('Sign-in was cancelled');\n\n  const { accessToken, idToken, code, expiresIn, error } = parseFragment(redirect);\n  if (error) throw new Error(`Google sign-in error: ${error}`);\n  if (!idToken || !accessToken) throw new Error('Sign-in did not return tokens');\n\n  const email = emailFromIdToken(idToken);\n  const bundle: TokenBundle = {\n    idToken,\n    accessToken,\n    email,\n    expiresAt: Date.now() + (expiresIn - 60) * 1000, // refresh 60s early\n  };\n  await chrome.storage.session.set({ [KEY]: bundle });\n  // Persist the email durably so a later silent renew (after a restart wipes the\n  // session bundle) still has a login_hint to pass.\n  if (email) await chrome.storage.local.set({ [EMAIL_KEY]: email });\n  return { bundle, code: code || undefined };\n}\n\n/** Durable email hint for silent renew (survives the restart that wipes the\n *  storage.session token bundle). */\nasync function readStoredEmail(): Promise<string | undefined> {\n  const r = await chrome.storage.local.get(EMAIL_KEY);\n  return (r[EMAIL_KEY] as string) ?? undefined;\n}"
}
```

```json
  "new_string": "export async function getValidTokens(): Promise<TokenBundle> {\n  const cached = await readCache();\n  if (cached && cached.expiresAt > Date.now()) return cached;\n  // prompt=none silent renew, passing the account hint so it works on a\n  // multi-account profile. cached?.email covers the common case; the durable copy\n  // covers a post-restart renew where the session bundle is gone.\n  const hint = cached?.email ?? (await readStoredEmail());\n  try {\n    return (await mint(false, hint)).bundle;\n  } catch (err) {\n    console.warn('[auxilio] silent token renew failed', err);\n    throw err;\n  }\n}"
}
```

```json
  "content": "---\nname: project_extension_sync_bounded_window\ndescription: Extension sync miss root cause was unbounded recurring expansion (6041 items/14s scan) + full-rescan-on-every-reload, NOT timeMin/wipe. Fixed with timeMax + config-gated rescan.\nmetadata:\n  type: project\n---\n\nauxilio-extension v0.10.0. The \"No upcoming visitor events\" / titleless nudge / empty sent-list cluster had ONE root cause, confirmed empirically in the background service-worker console:\n\n`events.list(singleEvents=true)` had **no `timeMax`**, so every recurring series expanded across ~2 years. A daily \"Lunch\" alone → ~700 instances; the user's calendar returned **6034 items / 25 pages / ~14.3s** per full scan. Two things made that fatal: (1) `marked` is only written AFTER the whole paginated loop, and MV3 kills the worker before 14s reliably completes → scan abandoned, `marked` stale/empty; (2) commit 629fae4 ran `clearSyncToken()` on every `onInstalled` (= every reload), so the expensive scan re-ran constantly during dev, keeping `marked` empty in the window. With `timeMax=now+90d`: **527 items / 3 pages / ~1.7s** (proven via console), and still finds the visitor event.\n\n**Disproven theory (don't re-derive it):** it was NOT `timeMin=now` excluding the event, and NOT a destructive wipe losing it — `markedTotal:1` showed the event DID get captured once a scan completed. The problem was scan *latency/fragility*, not exclusion.\n\nFixes shipped (v0.10.0):\n- **G1** `calendar.ts`: `timeMax = now + 90 days` (FORWARD_WINDOW_MS) on the tokenless branch only (timeMin/timeMax can't combine with syncToken; token preserves the window; 12h re-scan slides it).\n- **G2** `calendar-sync.ts` `syncConfigChanged()` + `SYNC_SCHEMA_VERSION=2` + `CONFIG_SIG_KEY`: `onInstalled` only drops the token when magic-address/schema changed; a plain reload resumes incremental sync. Bumping SYNC_SCHEMA_VERSION forces one clean re-scan.\n\n**How to apply:** if sync misses an event, check the background console `[auxilio] sync done {itemsReturned, markedTotal}` and item count FIRST — a huge itemsReturned = window/recurring-expansion problem, not capture logic. Don't remove timeMax or revert to clearSyncToken-on-every-reload. Related: [[project_extension_nudge_reliability]], [[project_extension_homescreen_management_surface]].\n"
}
```

> AGENT

All implemented, type-checks and builds clean (v0.10.0). Here's the summary.

## What shipped (v0.9.0 → v0.10.0)

**G1 + G2 — the empirically-confirmed core fix**
- `calendar.ts`: bounded the change-feed scan with **`timeMax = now + 90 days`** (tokenless branch only — `timeMin/timeMax` can't combine with a sync token). Your console test proved this cuts 6,034 items / 25 pages / **14.3s → 527 / 3 pages / 1.7s**, still finding the event.
- `calendar-sync.ts` + `background.ts`: **`onInstalled` no longer full-rescans on every reload.** `syncConfigChanged()` only drops the token when the magic address or query schema changed; a plain reload resumes cheap incremental sync. `SYNC_SCHEMA_VERSION = 2` forces exactly **one** clean re-scan on this first load to establish the bounded window.

**1A + 1B — title-flash polish** (`calendar.content.ts`)
- The optimistic DOM nudge no longer fires **without a title** (it waits the ~1.7s for the named API nudge instead of flashing nameless).
- The nudge render now shows a complete sentence even if a title is ever missing — no dangling empty slot.

**#2 — edit-revert / auth cluster**
- **2C (root)** `auth.ts`: silent `prompt=none` renew now passes **`login_hint`** (durably stored email) — the likely reason writes were failing on a multi-account profile, where Google can't pick an account non-interactively. Failures are now logged.
- **2A** `RosterRow.tsx`: name/phone now **commit as you type** (500ms debounce, blur flushes) — the Review button activates without waiting for blur, and the edit persists sooner so it can't be lost on a re-render.
- **2B** `hooks.ts` + `VisitPanel.tsx`: an auth-failed edit is **no longer silently rolled back**; a clear "Your session expired — Sign in" banner appears so the host can re-auth and re-send, instead of a mysterious revert.
- **2D** `VisitPanel.tsx`: the post-save `REFRESH_ACTIVE` refetch is **skipped while a write is in flight**, closing the race where a `DRAFT_LOAD` could overwrite an in-flight edit.

## To verify

1. **Reload the extension** (it shows **0.10.0**). This load triggers the one-time schema re-scan — in the background console you should see `[auxilio] sync done { mode: "full" … }` complete in ~2s, then `markedTotal` includes your event.
2. **Reload again** — this time you should see `mode: "incremental"` (no expensive full scan), confirming G2.
3. Create a visitor event → the nudge should show **with the name** (or briefly nothing, then named), never nameless.
4. On a sent event, edit a guest name → the button activates as you type and the value sticks.

## Honest caveat on #2

The sync issue I confirmed empirically; **#2's trigger I did not** — it's a strong hypothesis (silent-renew failing → silent rollback). 2C/2A/2D should prevent it, and 2B makes any residual failure visible instead of silent. If you still see a revert, open the **side-panel** console, reproduce, and check whether the `DRAFT_PATCH` RPC comes back with `needsAuth`/401 or `[auxilio] silent token renew failed` — that'll tell us if it's still the token path and we tune from there.

I also corrected the memory: the earlier "wipe / timeMin" theory was wrong, and that's now recorded so it doesn't get re-derived.

```json
{

  "content": "# Calendar Extension — Sync Visibility + Edit/Auth Fixes (v0.10.0)\n\n**Date:** 2026-06-24\n**Scope:** `auxilio-extension` only (the desktop Chrome surface). Resolves a cluster\nof reports: a nameless in-page nudge, \"No upcoming visitor events found\" on the\nhomescreen, an empty \"Passes sent\" list, and a sent-event guest edit that fails to\nactivate the Review button and then reverts. Includes the empirical confirmation\nprotocol, a **disproven** first hypothesis, the confirmed root cause, the product\ndecisions taken, and the shipped fixes.\n**Type:** Bug analysis + fix. **Implemented 2026-06-24 in extension v0.10.0.**\n**Related (read alongside):**\n- `2026-06-23_extension_visitor_event_sync_visibility_bug.md` (the v0.6.1 work this\n  supersedes/extends — its §5 \"open item\" is now resolved).\n- `2026-06-22_calendar_visitor_pass_status_bugs_analysis.md` (server-side pass status).\n- Memory: `project_extension_sync_bounded_window`, `project_extension_homescreen_management_surface`.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Reported symptoms\n\n1. **Nameless nudge.** Creating an event with the magic address showed the in-page\n   banner as just **\"Visitor event needs passes.\" + Manage** — no event name. The\n   reporter noted it *used to* show the name; \"recent changes\" broke it.\n2. **Homescreen empty.** After creating an event and sending passes, refreshing and\n   reopening the panel showed **\"No upcoming visitor events found. They appear here\n   once an event includes the visitor address.\"**\n3. **Empty \"Passes sent\" list.** The reporter could not see sent visitor events.\n4. **Sent-event edit is broken.** On an event whose passes were already sent: opening\n   the guest edit, changing a field **does not activate** the Review & send button;\n   on clicking away it **activates for ~1s then deactivates**, and the change has\n   **reverted to the original**.\n5. **Auth token expiry / re-issue** was an ongoing pain referenced throughout.\n6. **Design question:** with two nudge sources (instant DOM reader vs slower\n   Calendar-API sync), if the user acts on the DOM nudge and then the API data\n   arrives, are they nudged twice or is it resolved internally?\n\n---\n\n## 2. How the surfaces are fed (relevant data paths)\n\n- **Homescreen list** ← `LIST_VISITOR_EVENTS` → `runSync()` then `listForPanel()`\n  (v0.9.0+ management surface; reads the background **`marked`** set).\n- **Toolbar badge / OS notification / in-page named nudge** ← background sync →\n  `listMarked()` → `broadcastNudge()` (also reads `marked`, but **filters out**\n  `handled`/`engineHandled` events — the \"stop nagging once done\" overlay).\n- **In-page nudge** ← the sync set **∪ an optimistic DOM guess** captured by the\n  content script when an open event's visible text contains the magic address\n  (`calendar.content.ts` `render()` → `nudge.addOptimistic`). Merged by `eventId`,\n  sync wins (`merged()`).\n- **`marked` set** ← written **only** by the background list-sync (`doRunSync`,\n  `calendar-sync.ts`). The panel's own `events.get` (resolve/send) does **not** seed it.\n- **Auth** ← `getValidTokens()` returns a cached access token or silently re-mints via\n  `launchWebAuthFlow(interactive:false)` (`auth.ts`). Writes (`DRAFT_PATCH`, `SEND`)\n  go through `withTokens` / `withIdToken`.\n\nKey consequence used throughout the diagnosis: **everything the user \"can't see\"\n(named nudge, homescreen list, sent list) is downstream of the `marked` set.** If an\nevent isn't in `marked`, all three go dark together while the panel can still open and\nsend it (it uses `events.get` directly).\n\n---\n\n## 3. First hypothesis — and why it was WRONG\n\nInitial reading of the code pointed at commit `629fae4` (2026-06-23), which:\n\n- calls `clearSyncToken()` on **every** `onInstalled` (install/update/**reload**) →\n  forces a full tokenless re-scan, and\n- rebuilds `marked` from scratch on a full sync (`fullSync ? {} : {...prevMarked}`),\n- with the tokenless `events.list` using **`timeMin = now`** (forward-only).\n\nThe hypothesis: a reload **wipes** `marked` and rebuilds it from one forward-only\nlist call, so an already-started / all-day-today event (excluded by `timeMin=now`) is\n**dropped** — a destructive miss that worsened on every reload.\n\n**This was disproven by the empirical test (next section).** The event WAS being\ncaptured (`markedTotal: 1`), and a wider `timeMin` window did not change that. The\nproblem was scan **latency/fragility**, not exclusion or a destructive wipe. Recording\nthis explicitly so it is not re-derived.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Empirical confirmation (background service-worker console)\n\nRun from `chrome://extensions` → Auxilio → \"Inspect views: service worker\". No code\nchanges; existing diagnostics + ad-hoc Calendar API calls.\n\n**Step 2 — the sync's own diagnostic after a reload:**\n```\n[auxilio] sync done {\n  mode: \"full\", magicAddress: \"findajx@gmail.com\",\n  itemsReturned: 6041, markedTotal: 1, newMarked: 0\n}\n```\n→ The event *is* captured (`markedTotal: 1`). But **6041 items** were returned.\n\n**Step 3 — what `events.list(singleEvents=true)` actually returns**, timed and\npaginated both unbounded and bounded:\n\n| Window | Items | Pages | Time | Finds visitor event? |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| **Unbounded** (current) | 6034 | 25 | **14,298 ms** | yes (`Meeting 20`) |\n| **`timeMax = now + 90d`** | 527 | 3 | **1,737 ms** | yes (`Meeting 20`) |\n\nThe list was dominated by a **daily recurring \"Lunch\"** event expanded by\n`singleEvents=true` into hundreds of instances out to 2027. Loaded extension version\nconfirmed **0.9.0** (so the management-surface code was present — not a missing feature).\n\n---\n\n## 5. Confirmed root cause\n\n`events.list(singleEvents=true)` was called with **no `timeMax`** (`calendar.ts`), so\nevery recurring series expanded across Google's full ~2-year horizon. A busy calendar\nreturns **6000+ items**, a **~14s, 25-page** scan. Two facts make that fatal:\n\n1. **`marked` is written only AFTER the whole paginated loop finishes**\n   (`calendar-sync.ts`). **MV3 kills idle/long-running service workers** (~30s idle,\n   5-min hard cap) — a 14s fetch loop can be terminated mid-flight, so the scan is\n   abandoned with nothing saved and `marked` stays stale/empty.\n2. **`629fae4` ran this full scan on every reload** (`clearSyncToken` on\n   `onInstalled`). During active development (constant reloads) the user repeatedly\n   sat in the multi-second window where `marked` is empty/mid-rebuild.\n\nSo during that window: homescreen reads empty `marked` → **\"No upcoming visitor\nevents\"**; the named nudge (from `marked`) hasn't populated → only the **titleless\noptimistic DOM nudge** shows; the sent list is empty. When a scan *does* survive to\ncompletion, the event appears (hence `markedTotal: 1`). The intermittency is exactly\nthe \"it used to work, now it doesn't\" feeling — and `629fae4` amplified it by running\nthe expensive scan far more often, **not** by excluding the event via `timeMin`.\n\nThis single root cause explains symptoms **1, 2, and 3**. Symptoms **4/5 (edit/auth)**\nare a separate subsystem (§7). Symptom **6** is answered in §8.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Decisions taken\n\n- **D1 — Homescreen is a management surface (decided earlier in the thread, kept).**\n  `LIST_VISITOR_EVENTS` → `listForPanel()` lists pending **and** already-sent visitor\n  events (status-tagged: \"Visitor events needing passes\" / \"Passes sent\"). The *nag*\n  surfaces (badge, notification, in-page banner) still use `listMarked()` and stay\n  suppressed once handled. This is why \"empty after send\" was, in part, deliberate\n  suppression — but §5 showed the deeper cause was the event missing from `marked`.\n- **D2 — Bound the sync window rather than redesign recurring handling.** `timeMax`\n  is the surgical fix; dropping `singleEvents=true` (master-only) would lose\n  per-instance times and recurringEventId handling. Window = **90 days** (visitor\n  nudges are near-term; 90d → ~hundreds of rows / ~2s, comfortably inside the worker's\n  life). Tunable.\n- **D3 — Stop full-rescanning on every reload; gate on real config change.** Keep the\n  self-heal intent (a magic-address change must take effect) but trigger it only when\n  the config actually changes, via a stored signature + a bump-able schema version.\n- **D4 — Reject extra resilience (progressive `marked` writes / keep-alive).**\n  Unnecessary once the scan is ~1.7s; not worth the complexity.\n- **D5 — Scope of this change (user-selected):** implement **all** of: the core sync\n  fix (G1+G2), the title-flash polish (1A/1B), and the edit/auth cluster (#2).\n- **D6 — Intellectual-honesty caveat on #2:** the edit/auth trigger was a *hypothesis*,\n  not empirically confirmed like the sync issue. Ship the clearly-correct fixes and\n  make any residual failure **visible** rather than silent; give a console-confirmation\n  path if it recurs.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Solutions implemented (v0.10.0)\n\n### Core sync fix (confirmed)\n- **G1 — bounded window.** `lib/calendar.ts`: `FORWARD_WINDOW_MS = 90 days`;\n  the tokenless `events.list` now sets `timeMin = now` **and** `timeMax = now + 90d`.\n  `timeMin/timeMax` are incompatible with `syncToken`, so they are set only on the\n  tokenless branch; the token preserves the window, and the periodic 12h full re-scan\n  re-establishes it against a fresh `now` (sliding it forward).\n- **G2 — config-gated re-scan.** `lib/calendar-sync.ts`: `syncConfigChanged()` compares\n  a stored `CONFIG_SIG_KEY` (`${MAGIC_ADDRESS}|v${SYNC_SCHEMA_VERSION}`); `background.ts`\n  `onInstalled` drops the token **only** when it changed, else resumes incremental sync.\n  `SYNC_SCHEMA_VERSION = 2` forces exactly one clean re-scan to establish the bounded\n  window on first load after this change.\n\n### Title-flash polish\n- **1A** — `calendar.content.ts`: the optimistic DOM nudge no longer emits when the\n  title scrape returned empty; it waits for the (now ~1.7s) named API nudge. `syncBurst`\n  still fires.\n- **1B** — `calendar.content.ts`: the nudge render shows a complete self-contained\n  sentence (\"A visitor event needs passes.\") if a target ever has no title — never a\n  dangling empty name slot.\n\n### Edit / auth cluster (#2)\n- **2C (root)** — `lib/auth.ts`: the silent `prompt=none` renew now passes\n  **`login_hint`** (the connected account's email, stored durably in `EMAIL_KEY` so it\n  survives the restart that wipes the session bundle). Without a hint, `prompt=none`\n  fails on a multi-account profile (Google can't choose an account non-interactively),\n  which dead-ends every authed write. Renew failures are now logged. `signOut` clears\n  the stored email.\n- **2A** — `features/visit/RosterRow.tsx`: name/phone **commit as the host types**\n  (500ms debounce; blur flushes immediately). The Review button reflects the edit\n  without waiting for blur, and the value is persisted sooner so it can't be lost on a\n  re-render.\n- **2B** — `features/visit/hooks.ts` + `VisitPanel.tsx`: an auth-failed patch is **not\n  rolled back** (the typed value stays on screen); the panel shows a non-destructive\n  **\"Your session expired. Sign in again to save your changes.\"** banner with a Sign in\n  button. The generic send-error banner is suppressed for auth errors (the reconnect\n  banner covers them).\n- **2D** — `features/visit/VisitPanel.tsx`: the post-save `REFRESH_ACTIVE` refetch is\n  **skipped while a write is in flight** (`mutatingRef`), closing the race where a\n  `DRAFT_LOAD` could read the engine before the PATCH commits and overwrite the edit.\n\n### Housekeeping\n- Version bumped **0.9.0 → 0.10.0** (`package.json`; WXT syncs it to the manifest).\n- `npm run compile` (tsc) and `npm run build` both pass clean.\n\n---\n\n## 8. Answer to the DOM-vs-API double-nudge question (symptom 6)\n\n**Designed to resolve internally, and it does — as long as the two sources agree on\n`eventId`.** Both merge into one map keyed by `eventId` with the **sync entry winning**\n(`merged()`); acting on the nudge adds the `eventId` to a session `dismissed` set\n(persisted in `storage.session`), so the same id won't re-nudge this session even when\nthe slower sync target arrives later. Optimistic guesses also `dropOptimistic` on a\nmatching sync target and self-expire after a 12s TTL.\n\n**Failure mode = `eventId` mismatch** between the DOM `data-eventid` and the Calendar\nAPI `ev.id` — mainly **recurring instances** or any id-format drift. When ids differ,\nthe two entries don't collapse, and dismissing/handling one doesn't suppress the other\n(it can double-nudge). This is a **cross-cutting id-consistency** concern (also affects\n`markHandled` suppression and the sent-tag lookup). **Not fixed in v0.10.0** — see Open\nitems. For a single, non-recurring event with matching ids, there is no double nudge.\n\n---\n\n## 9. Verification\n\n1. Reload the extension (`chrome://extensions` shows **0.10.0**). First load triggers\n   the one-time schema re-scan: background console shows `[auxilio] sync done\n   { mode: \"full\" … }` completing in ~2s, `markedTotal` includes the event.\n2. Reload again → `mode: \"incremental\"` (no expensive full scan) — confirms G2.\n3. Create a visitor event → the nudge shows **with the name** (or briefly nothing, then\n   named) — never nameless.\n4. On a sent event, edit a guest name → the button activates as you type and the value\n   sticks (no revert).\n5. If a revert recurs: open the **side-panel** console, reproduce, and check whether the\n   `DRAFT_PATCH` RPC returns `needsAuth`/401 or logs `[auxilio] silent token renew\n   failed` — tells us if it's still the token path.\n\n---\n\n## 10. Open items / deferred\n\n- **id-consistency (symptom 6 root).** Make the optimistic path use the same canonical\n  event id as the sync/API (or seed `marked` from the panel's authoritative `events.get`\n  — the earlier \"3A/4A\" idea) so merge/dedup/handled/sent all line up for recurring\n  events. Deferred; low observed impact for single events.\n- **#2 trigger not empirically confirmed.** 2C is the most likely root fix; 2A/2B/2D are\n  supporting and make any residual failure visible. Revisit with side-panel console logs\n  if it recurs.\n- **2B is surface-not-replay.** After re-auth, a failed name patch may need re-touching\n  the field (blur re-commits) before send — the engine materializes from its stored\n  draft, so the patch must land before send. Full preserve-and-replay was deemed out of\n  scope for a hypothesized bug.\n- **Secondary calendars** remain deferred (primary-only sync; no `calendarlist` scope) —\n  unchanged from prior docs.\n- **Window size (90d)** is a guess balancing coverage vs scan cost; revisit if a\n  legitimate >90-day-out visitor event is ever missed (it would surface once the window\n  slides within range).\n"
```

The patch RPC runs panel → background → engine, and the engine round-trip is logged in the **background** console. That single line tells us whether the write failed (→ rollback → revert) or succeeded (→ a client-side clobber).

## Please capture this

1. Open **two** consoles:
   - **Background:** `chrome://extensions` → Auxilio → "Inspect views: service worker".
   - **Side panel:** right-click inside the open side panel → **Inspect**.
2. Reproduce cleanly: open the **sent** event → edit a guest's name → click away → watch it revert.
3. From the **background** console, report the two lines around the edit:
   ```
   [auxilio] engine → PATCH …/api/visitor/calendar/addon/draft
   [auxilio] engine ← <status> <ms> <body…>
   ```
4. From the **side-panel** console, report any red errors (and anything starting with `[auxilio]`).

## What each outcome means (so we know the fix immediately)

| Background PATCH line | Meaning | Direction |
|---|---|---|
| `← 404` (`draft not found`) | The iCalUid the panel holds doesn't match the stored draft's key (key drift on a materialized event) | Fix: resolve/rekey on patch |
| `← 0` (timeout / "could not reach") or `← 500` | Transport/server failure → `onError` rolls back → revert | Fix: retry / don't hard-rollback |
| **`← 200`** but value still reverts | Patch succeeded; something **client-side** overwrites it — most likely `useResolveGuestNames` (People API) re-applying a contacts name over your edit, since the engine's `patchDraft` doesn't clear `nameIsFallback` (`visit-drafts.ts:699`), so the guest stays "pending" for name resolution | Fix: clear `nameIsFallback` on edit / don't clobber edited fields |
| No PATCH line appears at all | The debounced commit isn't firing | Fix: the commit wiring |

My current lead — assuming you'll see **`← 200`** — is that the contacts name-resolver (`useResolveGuestNames` in `hooks.ts`) is racing your edit: the engine keeps `nameIsFallback: true` after a name patch, so the People API merge re-runs and writes the resolved/original name back over what you typed. But I want the log to confirm it before I touch anything, rather than repeat the earlier mistake of fixing a theory.

```
1	/**
2	 * People API name + photo resolution (background only).
3	 *
4	 * Why: Google omits `attendee.displayName` for external non-contact guests — the
5	 * name Calendar's web UI shows is resolved client-side from the host's Google
6	 * Contacts and never reaches the event API. We replicate that with the host's own
7	 * `access_token` (the same client-side pattern the Calendar add-on uses), so the
8	 * side panel can upgrade a guest's email-derived fallback name (and show a profile
9	 * photo) with no backend round-trip.
10	 *
11	 * Needs contacts.readonly + contacts.other.readonly on the OAuth consent (see
12	 * lib/auth SCOPES). Resolves only emails the host has saved or interacted with —
13	 * fails gracefully (returns nothing) for everyone else. Never throws.
14	 */
15	import type { ResolvedPerson } from './types';
16	
17	const PEOPLE_BASE = 'https://people.googleapis.com/v1';
18	const CACHE_KEY = 'auxilio.peopleCache';
19	const CACHE_TTL_MS = 7 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000; // names/photos change rarely
20	const CACHE_MAX = 1000;
21	
22	type CacheEntry = { person: ResolvedPerson | null; at: number };
23	
24	// In-memory cache for this service-worker's life (null = looked up, not found).
25	// MV3 workers are short-lived, so we also persist to storage.local below.
26	const mem = new Map<string, ResolvedPerson | null>();
27	let warmed = false;
28	
29	interface PeoplePerson {
30	  names?: { displayName?: string }[];
31	  emailAddresses?: { value?: string }[];
32	  photos?: { url?: string; default?: boolean }[];
33	}
34	
35	async function search(
36	  path: string,
37	  query: string,
38	  readMask: string,
39	  accessToken: string,
40	): Promise<{ results?: { person?: PeoplePerson }[] }> {
41	  const url = new URL(`${PEOPLE_BASE}/${path}`);
42	  url.searchParams.set('query', query);
43	  url.searchParams.set('readMask', readMask);
44	  url.searchParams.set('pageSize', '5');
45	  const res = await fetch(url, { headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${accessToken}` } });
46	  if (!res.ok) throw new Error(`People ${path} ${res.status}`);
47	  return res.json();
48	}
49	
50	/** Google primes the search index only after a prior request, so an empty-query
51	 *  call must precede the first real one. Once per worker life. */
52	async function warmup(accessToken: string): Promise<void> {
53	  if (warmed) return;
54	  warmed = true;
55	  await Promise.all([
56	    search('people:searchContacts', '', 'names,emailAddresses', accessToken).catch(() => undefined),
57	    search('otherContacts:search', '', 'names,emailAddresses', accessToken).catch(() => undefined),
58	  ]);
59	}
60	
61	/** A result only counts if it actually carries the email we searched (the search is
62	 *  fuzzy). Skip Google's generic silhouette photo (`default: true`). */
63	function pick(person: PeoplePerson | undefined, email: string): ResolvedPerson | undefined {
64	  if (!person) return undefined;
65	  const emails = person.emailAddresses ?? [];
66	  if (!emails.some((e) => e.value?.toLowerCase() === email.toLowerCase())) return undefined;
67	  const name = person.names?.[0]?.displayName?.trim();
68	  const photo = (person.photos ?? []).find((p) => p.url && !p.default);
69	  const out: ResolvedPerson = {};
70	  if (name) out.name = name;
71	  if (photo?.url) out.photoUrl = photo.url;
72	  return out.name || out.photoUrl ? out : undefined;
73	}
74	
75	async function resolveOne(email: string, accessToken: string): Promise<ResolvedPerson | null> {
76	  try {
77	    const own = await search('people:searchContacts', email, 'names,emailAddresses,photos', accessToken);
78	    for (const r of own.results ?? []) {
79	      const hit = pick(r.person, email);
80	      if (hit) return hit;
81	    }
82	  } catch (err) {
83	    console.warn('[auxilio] searchContacts failed', email, err);
84	  }
85	  try {
86	    // otherContacts readMask does not reliably support photos → names only.
87	    const other = await search('otherContacts:search', email, 'names,emailAddresses', accessToken);
88	    for (const r of other.results ?? []) {
89	      const hit = pick(r.person, email);
90	      if (hit) return hit;
91	    }
92	  } catch (err) {
93	    console.warn('[auxilio] otherContacts.search failed', email, err);
94	  }
95	  return null;
96	}
97	
98	/**
99	 * Resolve real name + photo for the given guest emails. Returns only the ones we
100	 * found something for. Cached (memory + storage.local with a TTL), looked up in
101	 * parallel, and best-effort — a failure for one email never rejects the whole call.
102	 */
103	export async function resolveGuests(
104	  accessToken: string,
105	  emails: string[],
106	): Promise<Record<string, ResolvedPerson>> {
107	  const want = [...new Set(emails.map((e) => e.toLowerCase()).filter(Boolean))];
108	  if (want.length === 0) return {};
109	
110	  const persisted = await loadCache();
111	  const out: Record<string, ResolvedPerson> = {};
112	  const todo: string[] = [];
113	
114	  for (const email of want) {
115	    const m = mem.get(email);
116	    if (m !== undefined) {
117	      if (m) out[email] = m;
118	      continue;
119	    }
120	    const p = persisted[email];
121	    if (p && Date.now() - p.at < CACHE_TTL_MS) {
122	      mem.set(email, p.person);
123	      if (p.person) out[email] = p.person;
124	      continue;
125	    }
126	    todo.push(email);
127	  }
128	
129	  if (todo.length > 0) {
130	    await warmup(accessToken);
131	    const results = await Promise.all(todo.map((e) => resolveOne(e, accessToken)));
132	    todo.forEach((email, i) => {
133	      const person = results[i];
134	      mem.set(email, person);
135	      persisted[email] = { person, at: Date.now() };
136	      if (person) out[email] = person;
137	    });
138	    await saveCache(persisted);
139	  }
140	  return out;
141	}
142	
143	async function loadCache(): Promise<Record<string, CacheEntry>> {
144	  try {
145	    const r = await chrome.storage.local.get(CACHE_KEY);
146	    return (r[CACHE_KEY] as Record<string, CacheEntry>) ?? {};
147	  } catch {
148	    return {};
149	  }
150	}
151	
152	async function saveCache(cache: Record<string, CacheEntry>): Promise<void> {
153	  try {
154	    // Drop expired entries, then cap to the most recent CACHE_MAX so the store
155	    // can't grow without bound.
156	    const now = Date.now();
157	    let entries = Object.entries(cache).filter(([, v]) => now - v.at < CACHE_TTL_MS);
158	    if (entries.length > CACHE_MAX) {
159	      entries = entries.sort((a, b) => b[1].at - a[1].at).slice(0, CACHE_MAX);
160	    }
161	    await chrome.storage.local.set({ [CACHE_KEY]: Object.fromEntries(entries) });
162	  } catch {
163	    /* best-effort cache */
164	  }
165	}
166
```

> AGENT

PATCH 200 + no refetch = a **client-side clobber**, and I traced the only remaining cache-writer: **`useResolveGuestNames`** (the People API contacts resolver). Here's the mechanism:

1. You edit the name → engine persists it (200), but `patchDraft` leaves `nameIsFallback: true` (`visit-drafts.ts:699` never clears it).
2. So the guest stays "pending" for name resolution. A contacts lookup that was **in flight from when the panel loaded** (People API `warmup` + search is ~1s — matches your "active for a second") returns, and `useResolveGuestNames` writes the **contacts name** back into the draft cache (`hooks.ts:132`), overwriting your edit.
3. `RosterRow`'s reconciliation then adopts that cache value because, post-save, `local === lastServerName` — so it can't tell your just-saved edit from a fresh server value, and reverts the field.

The resolver overwriting a real/edited name is simply wrong — it should only *fill* a missing (fallback) name. Let me fix that, cancel the in-flight resolve on edit, and make it durable on the engine — plus a confirming log so your next test proves the mechanism.

**Client: only fill fallback names + clear the flag on edit (`hooks.ts`):**
