118	export function useResolveGuestNames(draft: DraftResponse | undefined) {
119	  const qc = useQueryClient();
120	  const iCalUid = draft?.iCalUid;
121	  const pending = (draft?.roster ?? [])
122	    .filter((g) => g.nameIsFallback)
123	    .map((g) => g.email.toLowerCase())
124	    .sort();
125	  // Stable key so the effect fires only when the pending set actually changes.
126	  const key = pending.join('|');
127	
128	  useEffect(() => {
129	    if (!iCalUid || pending.length === 0) return;
130	    let cancelled = false;
131	    rpc({ type: 'RESOLVE_GUESTS', emails: pending })
132	      .then((resolved) => {
133	        if (cancelled || !resolved || Object.keys(resolved).length === 0) return;
134	        qc.setQueryData<DraftResponse>(['draft', iCalUid], (prev) => {
135	          if (!prev) return prev;
136	          return {
137	            ...prev,
138	            roster: prev.roster.map((g) => {
139	              const hit = resolved[g.email.toLowerCase()];
140	              if (!hit) return g;
141	              return {
142	                ...g,
143	                name: hit.name ?? g.name,
144	                photoUrl: hit.photoUrl ?? g.photoUrl,
145	                // A real name resolved → it's no longer a fallback.
146	                nameIsFallback: hit.name ? false : g.nameIsFallback,
147	              };
148	            }),
149	          };
150	        });
151	      })
152	      .catch(() => {
153	        /* name resolution is best-effort — failures keep the fallback name */
154	      });
155	    return () => {
156	      cancelled = true;
157	    };
158	    // eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps
159	  }, [iCalUid, key]);
160	}

  "old_string": "function applyPatch(draft: DraftResponse, patch: DraftPatch): DraftResponse {\n  const roster = draft.roster.map((g) => {\n    const edit = patch.guests?.find(\n      (e) => e.email.toLowerCase() === g.email.toLowerCase(),\n    );\n    return edit ? { ...g, ...stripEmail(edit) } : g;\n  });\n  return {\n    ...draft,\n    roster,\n    location: patch.location ?? draft.location,\n  };\n}",
  "new_string": "function applyPatch(draft: DraftResponse, patch: DraftPatch): DraftResponse {\n  const roster = draft.roster.map((g) => {\n    const edit = patch.guests?.find(\n      (e) => e.email.toLowerCase() === g.email.toLowerCase(),\n    );\n    if (!edit) return g;\n    const next = { ...g, ...stripEmail(edit) };\n    // A host-typed name is authoritative: stop marking it a fallback so the\n    // contacts resolver no longer targets it — and changing the fallback set\n    // cancels any in-flight resolve for this guest (the effect key changes).\n    if (typeof edit.name === 'string' && edit.name.trim()) next.nameIsFallback = false;\n    return next;\n  });\n  return {\n    ...draft,\n    roster,\n    location: patch.location ?? draft.location,\n  };\n}"
}

211	
212	## 8. Answer to the DOM-vs-API double-nudge question (symptom 6)
213	
214	**Designed to resolve internally, and it does — as long as the two sources agree on
215	`eventId`.** Both merge into one map keyed by `eventId` with the **sync entry winning**
216	(`merged()`); acting on the nudge adds the `eventId` to a session `dismissed` set
217	(persisted in `storage.session`), so the same id won't re-nudge this session even when
218	the slower sync target arrives later. Optimistic guesses also `dropOptimistic` on a
219	matching sync target and self-expire after a 12s TTL.
220	
221	**Failure mode = `eventId` mismatch** between the DOM `data-eventid` and the Calendar
222	API `ev.id` — mainly **recurring instances** or any id-format drift. When ids differ,
223	the two entries don't collapse, and dismissing/handling one doesn't suppress the other
224	(it can double-nudge). This is a **cross-cutting id-consistency** concern (also affects
225	`markHandled` suppression and the sent-tag lookup). **Not fixed in v0.10.0** — see Open
226	items. For a single, non-recurring event with matching ids, there is no double nudge.
227	
228	---
229	
230	## 9. Verification
231	
232	1. Reload the extension (`chrome://extensions` shows **0.10.0**). First load triggers
233	   the one-time schema re-scan: background console shows `[auxilio] sync done
234	   { mode: "full" … }` completing in ~2s, `markedTotal` includes the event.
235	2. Reload again → `mode: "incremental"` (no expensive full scan) — confirms G2.

Edit-revert — real root cause + fix (v0.10.1)\n\nThe v0.10.0 auth fixes (2A–2D) did **not** stop the revert. Empirical capture from the\n**background** console during the repro (edit a sent event's guest name → revert):\n\n```\n[auxilio] engine → PATCH …/addon/draft\n[auxilio] engine ← 200 236ms {…}            ← the write SUCCEEDS\n(no POST /addon/draft afterwards)            ← no refetch\n(no red errors in the side panel)\n```\n\nPATCH `200` + **no refetch** ⇒ a **client-side cache clobber**, not a server/auth/race\nfailure. Tracing the only remaining writer of the draft cache pinned it to the **People\nAPI contacts resolver** (`useResolveGuestNames`, `hooks.ts`):\n\n1. The host edits the name → engine persists it (`200`), but `patchDraft` left\n   **`nameIsFallback: true`** (`visit-drafts.ts`) — it never cleared the flag.\n2. So the guest stayed \"pending\" for name resolution. A contacts lookup **already\n   in flight from panel load** (People API `warmup` + search ≈ 1s — matches the\n   \"active for a second\" timing) returned and wrote the **contacts name** back into\n   the draft cache, overwriting the edit (`hooks.ts` resolve merge).\n3. `RosterRow`'s reconciliation then **adopted** that cache value: post-save,\n   `local === lastServerName`, so it could not distinguish the just-saved edit from a\n   fresh server value and reverted the field.\n\nThe resolver overwriting a *real/edited* name is simply wrong — it must only **fill** a\nmissing (fallback) name.\n\n**Fix (v0.10.1):**\n- `hooks.ts` `useResolveGuestNames`: only fills the name when the guest is still a\n  fallback (`fill = g.nameIsFallback`); a real/edited name is never overwritten (the\n  photo may still upgrade). Added a `[auxilio] resolveGuests applied for …` diagnostic.\n- `hooks.ts` `applyPatch` (optimistic): a host-typed non-empty name sets\n  `nameIsFallback = false`, which also **cancels any in-flight resolve** for that guest\n  (the effect's `key` changes → cleanup sets `cancelled = true`).\n- `hooks.ts` `usePatchDraft.onSuccess`: preserves `nameIsFallback = false` for the\n  just-named guests, because the engine echoes the OLD flag and would otherwise re-arm\n  the resolver before the engine fix is deployed.\n- **Engine** `visit-drafts.ts` `patchDraft`: clears `nameIsFallback` when a non-empty\n  name is patched — the **durable** fix so a reload doesn't re-target the guest. (Needs\n  the local engine to pick up the change — `bun --watch` / restart; the extension talks\n  to it via the ngrok tunnel in `.env`.)\n\n**Lesson (recorded):** two of three hypotheses on this bug were wrong until forced\nthrough console evidence (sync: disproved wipe/timeMin; edit: disproved auth). The\ndiscipline of \"reproduce in the background/side-panel console, read the actual\nstatus/sequence, *then* fix\" is what isolated each real cause. Default to it here.\n\n**Verify (v0.10.1):** edit a sent guest's name, click away → it sticks (no revert); the\nbutton stays active. The side-panel console may log `[auxilio] resolveGuests applied\nfor [...]` — with the guard, it no longer changes the edited name. Reload → the name\npersists (confirms the engine flag fix is live)."
}

> AGENT

Done. The console capture nailed it — **PATCH `200` + no refetch = a client-side cache clobber, not auth.** My #2 auth fix was the wrong tree; here's the real one.

## Root cause (confirmed by your log)

The **contacts name-resolver** was overwriting your edit:

1. You edit the name → engine persists it (`200`), but `patchDraft` left `nameIsFallback: true` — it never cleared the flag.
2. So the guest stays "pending" for name resolution. A People API contacts lookup **already in flight from when the panel loaded** (warmup + search ≈ 1s — exactly your "active for a second") returns and writes the **contacts name** back into the draft cache, over your edit.
3. `RosterRow`'s reconciliation then adopts that cache value — post-save, `local === lastServerName`, so it can't tell your saved edit from a fresh server value, and reverts the field.

The resolver overwriting a *real/edited* name is just wrong; it should only **fill** a missing name.

## Fix (v0.10.1)

- **`hooks.ts` resolver** — only fills a name when the guest is still a fallback; never overwrites a real/edited name (photo can still upgrade). Added a `[auxilio] resolveGuests applied for …` log.
- **`hooks.ts` `applyPatch`** — a host-typed name clears `nameIsFallback` optimistically, which also **cancels the in-flight resolve** for that guest (the effect key changes).
- **`hooks.ts` `onSuccess`** — keeps the flag cleared through the engine's echo (the engine still returned the old flag until its fix deploys).
- **Engine `visit-drafts.ts` `patchDraft`** — clears `nameIsFallback` when a name is set, so a **reload** doesn't re-target the guest (durable fix).

Extension compiles + builds clean (now **0.10.1**). I kept 2A–2D — they're correct hardening, just not this bug's cause.

## To verify

1. Reload the extension (shows **0.10.1**). **Restart your local engine** so the `patchDraft` change is live (the extension hits it via the ngrok tunnel).
2. Edit a sent guest's name → click away → **it sticks**, button stays active.
3. The side-panel console may log `[auxilio] resolveGuests applied for [...]` around the edit — with the guard, it no longer changes your name (that log confirms the resolver was the culprit).
4. Reload the panel → the name persists (confirms the engine flag fix).

If it *still* reverts, that `resolveGuests applied` log (or its absence) tells us immediately whether it's the resolver or something I haven't found — paste it and I'll keep going.

I also updated the planning doc (new **§11** with the real cause, and a correction marker on the auth section) and the memory, so the wrong auth hypothesis isn't left as the recorded answer.

One caveat worth flagging honestly: this is my third diagnosis on this thread, and the first two were wrong until console evidence forced the issue. I'm confident in the mechanism here because it's the only cache-writer left standing after `200`/no-refetch — but the confirming log is there precisely so you don't have to take that on faith.

The names are resolved **client-side only** (People API → React Query cache), so they're never persisted to the engine. When you send, `materializeDraft`/`applyDraft` reads `guest.name` from the **stored** draft — which still holds the email-derived fallback — so the pass and email use that.

The fix: when the resolver upgrades a fallback name, also persist it to the engine via a draft patch (which now also clears `nameIsFallback`). Let me confirm the current resolver code and the send path, then patch it.

Clean — built as **0.10.3**.

## What was wrong

Name resolution runs **client-side only** (extension → People API → React Query cache). The resolved name was shown in the UI but never sent to the engine, so the engine's stored draft kept the email-derived fallback — and `materializeDraft`/`applyDraft` (`visit-drafts.ts`) builds the pass and email from `guest.name`, i.e. the fallback.

## Fix (0.10.3)

In `useResolveGuestNames` (`hooks.ts`), when the resolver upgrades a fallback name, it now also **persists it to the engine** via `DRAFT_PATCH` (best-effort). That patch also clears `nameIsFallback` server-side (the engine change from 0.10.1), so the stored draft carries the real name and the sent pass/email use it.

## To verify

1. Reload the extension (**0.10.3**); make sure the local engine is running the updated `patchDraft`.
2. Open an event with a guest whose name resolves from contacts → send passes → the **pass and email** now show the real name, not the trimmed email.

Two honest caveats:
- **Already-sent invites won't change** — this only affects sends made after the name is resolved/persisted. If the host clicks Send within the ~1s before resolution completes, the fallback could still be used; resolution normally finishes well before review.
- The **add-on** (`auxilio-workspace`) likely has the same client-only-resolution gap. I scoped this fix to the extension since that's where you hit it — say the word and I'll check/mirror it there.

397	/**
398	 * Apply an edit to an already-created invite (the Google-Sheet edit-sync path).
399	 *
400	 * - Diffs each field against the stored invite; if nothing changed it is a
401	 *   no-op (no write, no email) — this is what keeps a routine re-Process from
402	 *   spuriously re-emailing guests.
403	 * - Guest-facing changes (name / email / purpose / date / time / host) resend
404	 *   the pass email with the "rescheduled / updated" copy.
405	 * - Host changes re-resolve the contact and reconcile stakeholders.
406	 * - Throws `InviteNotEditableError` for used/cancelled/rejected/expired invites.
407	 */
408	export async function updateVisitorInvite(
409	  invitationId: string,
410	  input: VisitorInviteUpdateInput,
411	): Promise<UpdateVisitorInviteResult> {
412	  const invitation = await getInvitationById(invitationId);
413	  if (!invitation) throw new Error('Invitation not found in system.');
414	
415	  if (invitation.token_used) {
416	    throw new InviteNotEditableError('This pass has already been used — edit it in the dashboard, not the sheet.');
417	  }
418	  if (['cancelled', 'rejected', 'expired'].includes(invitation.approval_status)) {

427	    changedFields.push('name');
428	  }
429	  if (input.visitorPhone !== undefined && _norm(input.visitorPhone) !== _norm(invitation.visitor_phone)) {
430	    fieldUpdates.visitorPhone = _norm(input.visitorPhone) || null;
431	    changedFields.push('phone');
432	  }
433	  if (input.visitorEmail !== undefined && _norm(input.visitorEmail) !== _norm(invitation.visitor_email)) {
434	    fieldUpdates.visitorEmail = _norm(input.visitorEmail) || null;

442	  // Date / time → recompute valid_from / valid_until. Compare on the IST HH:MM
443	  // projection of the stored ISO timestamps so a re-Process doesn't false-diff.
444	  const currentStart = _hhmm(invitation.valid_from);
445	  const currentEnd = _hhmm(invitation.valid_until);
446	  const dateChanged = input.expectedDate !== undefined && _norm(input.expectedDate) !== _norm(invitation.expected_date);
447	  const startChanged = input.startTime !== undefined && _norm(input.startTime) !== currentStart;
448	  const endChanged = input.endTime !== undefined && _norm(input.endTime) !== currentEnd;
449	
450	  const effectiveDate = input.expectedDate !== undefined ? _norm(input.expectedDate) : invitation.expected_date;
451	  const effectiveStart = input.startTime !== undefined ? _norm(input.startTime) : currentStart;
452	  const effectiveEnd = input.endTime !== undefined ? _norm(input.endTime) : currentEnd;
453	
454	  if (dateChanged) {
455	    fieldUpdates.expectedDate = effectiveDate;
456	    changedFields.push('date');
457	  }
458	  if (dateChanged || startChanged) {
459	    fieldUpdates.expectedTime = effectiveStart || null;
460	    fieldUpdates.validFrom = effectiveStart
461	      ? (combineISTDateAndTimeToISO(effectiveDate, effectiveStart) ?? undefined)
462	      : (startOfISTDayToISO(effectiveDate) ?? undefined);
463	    if (startChanged) changedFields.push('start time');
464	  }
465	  if (dateChanged || endChanged) {
466	    fieldUpdates.validUntil = effectiveEnd
467	      ? (combineISTDateAndTimeToISO(effectiveDate, effectiveEnd) ?? undefined)
468	      : (computeExpiry('invited', effectiveDate));
469	    if (endChanged) changedFields.push('end time');

> AGENT

`updateVisitorInvite` does exactly what's needed — diffs name/phone, updates the invitation, and resends the pass email. The gap is that `applyDraft` never calls it: it only **creates** (include + no pass) or **cancels** (exclude + has pass), so an edit to an already-issued guest falls through → "no changes". Let me read the full `applyDraft` and its result type to wire it in:

> TOOL

807	export interface ApplyResult {
808	  /** Every currently-active invitation id (for the ledger). */
809	  invitationIds: string[];
810	  /** Guests whose pass was created in THIS apply. */
811	  created: PassResult[];
812	  /** Guests whose pass was cancelled in THIS apply. */
813	  cancelled: { visitorEmail: string; invitationId: string }[];
814	  /** Guests we tried to send but couldn't (e.g. event time in the past). */
815	  failed: { visitorEmail: string; reason: string }[];
816	  draft: VisitDraft;
817	}
818	
819	/**
820	 * Reconcile the live passes to match the roster's include toggles (plan §1):
821	 *   include=true  & no active pass → create
822	 *   include=false & active pass    → cancel
823	 *   include=true  & active pass     → keep (a same-time edit; event-move reissue is
824	 *                                     handled separately in calendar-ingest)
825	 * Persists the draft (status + invitationId per guest, materialized flag). The
826	 * caller writes the `calendar_event_links` ledger from `invitationIds`.
827	 */
828	export async function applyDraft(draft: VisitDraft, opts: { hostName?: string } = {}): Promise<ApplyResult> {
829	  // Pass duration must equal the event window — never guess (decision 2026-06-17).
830	  // When the times are unavailable we still process cancellations, but refuse to
831	  // CREATE a pass with a wrong duration; those guests are reported as failed.
832	  const window = resolveEventWindow(draft);
833	  const start = window?.start;
834	  const end = window?.end;
835	  const resolvedHost = draft.hostEmail
836	    ? await resolveHostContactByEmail(draft.hostEmail).catch(() => null)
837	    : null;
838	  const hostName = await resolveHostDisplayName(draft.hostEmail, opts.hostName);
839	
840	  const created: PassResult[] = [];
841	  const cancelled: { visitorEmail: string; invitationId: string }[] = [];
842	  const failed: { visitorEmail: string; reason: string }[] = [];
843	
844	  for (const guest of draft.roster) {
845	    const hasActivePass = guest.status === 'sent' && !!guest.invitationId;
846	
847	    if (guest.include && !hasActivePass) {
848	      if (!start || !end) {
849	        // No reliable event window → don't issue a wrong-duration pass.
850	        failed.push({ visitorEmail: guest.email, reason: EVENT_TIME_MISSING });
851	        continue;
852	      }
853	      const visitorName = guest.name?.trim() || nameFromEmail(guest.email);
854	      try {
855	        const result = await createVisitorInvite({
856	          visitorName,
857	          visitorEmail: guest.email,
858	          visitorPhone: guest.phone || undefined,
859	          purpose: draft.title,
860	          hostEmail: draft.hostEmail,
861	          hostEmployeeId: resolvedHost?.staff?.id,
862	          hostEmployeeName: hostName ?? draft.hostEmail,
863	          expectedDate: istDate(start),
864	          expectedTime: istTime(start),
865	          validFrom: start.toISOString(),
866	          validUntil: end.toISOString(),
867	          emailTemplateKey: guest.emailTemplateKey,
868	          passTemplateKey: guest.passTemplateKey,
869	          invitedVia: 'calendar',
870	          invitedBy: draft.hostEmail,
871	          batchId: draft.iCalUid,
872	          approvalTier: 'invited',
873	          allowInternalColleague: true, // host-included guest = override (F2)
874	        });
875	        guest.invitationId = result.invitationId;
876	        guest.status = 'sent';
877	        created.push({ visitorEmail: guest.email, visitorName, invitationId: result.invitationId, passUrl: result.qrUrl, status: 'sent' });
878	      } catch (err) {
879	        const reason = err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err);
880	        console.warn(`[VisitDraft] apply-create for ${guest.email} failed:`, reason);
881	        failed.push({ visitorEmail: guest.email, reason });
882	      }
883	    } else if (!guest.include && hasActivePass) {
884	      const id = guest.invitationId as string;
885	      const ok = await cancelInvitationById(id).catch((e) => {
886	        console.warn(`[VisitDraft] apply-cancel ${id} failed:`, e);
887	        return false;
888	      });
889	      if (ok) await notifyInviteCancelled(id, { cancelledBy: hostName, reason: 'The host cancelled your visitor pass.' });
890	      cancelled.push({ visitorEmail: guest.email, invitationId: id });
891	      guest.status = 'cancelled';
892	      guest.invitationId = undefined;
893	    }
894	  }
895	
896	  const invitationIds = draft.roster
897	    .filter((g) => g.status === 'sent' && g.invitationId)

912	      const redirectUri = String(body.redirectUri ?? '').trim();
913	      if (!code || !redirectUri) { json(res, 422, { error: 'code and redirectUri required' }); return true; }
914	
915	      // Never downgrade a host who already has offline access (e.g. Slack rw connect) —
916	      // any connected refresh token already powers the read poll. (D7)
917	      const existing = await getCalendarConnection(identity.email).catch(() => null);

> AGENT

Engine builds clean — bug 1 is done. Now bug 3 (button flicker on initial load): that's **not** background processing. It's the content script racing Google Calendar's SPA hydration — when you refresh, Google tears down and rebuilds the event surface repeatedly, so `readSurface()` flips between "surface present" and "gone," toggling our button. It settles once hydration finishes. I can smooth it with a short hide-debounce so a transient "gone" during hydration doesn't flash the button away. Let me look at the button's `update`:

It's only there to surface the\n    // panel when we can't inject a native button; with the panel already open\n    // it's redundant (auto-follow handles the viewed event), and it would flash\n    // at the corner while a navigated page is still loading. So callers pass\n    // false when the panel is open.\n    update(show: boolean, allowFab = true) {\n      clearTimeout(retryTimer);\n      if (!show) {\n        removeInjected();\n        fab.setVisible(false);\n        return;\n      }",
  "new_string": "  // Debounce HIDING so a transient \"surface gone\" tick during Google's initial\n  // SPA hydration (it tears the event surface down and rebuilds it several times\n  // on a page refresh) doesn't flash the button away and back. We only remove the\n  // button if the surface stays gone for HIDE_GRACE_MS; a re-appearance cancels it.\n  let hideTimer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | undefined;\n  const HIDE_GRACE_MS = 400;\n\n  return {\n    // `allowFab` gates the floating fallback. It's only there to surface the\n    // panel when we can't inject a native button; with the panel already open\n    // it's redundant (auto-follow handles the viewed event), and it would flash\n    // at the corner while a navigated page is still loading. So callers pass\n    // false when the panel is open.\n    update(show: boolean, allowFab = true) {\n      clearTimeout(retryTimer);\n      if (!show) {\n        // Defer the hide: if the surface comes right back (hydration churn), the\n        // next update(true) cancels this and the button never visibly disappears.\n        clearTimeout(hideTimer);\n        hideTimer = setTimeout(() => {\n          removeInjected();\n          fab.setVisible(false);\n        }, HIDE_GRACE_MS);\n        return;\n      }\n      clearTimeout(hideTimer); // surface is present → cancel any pending hide"
}

> AGENT

Both build clean. Here's where each of the three stands.

## Bug 1 — edit → send said "no changes" ✅ Fixed

Confirmed root cause: `applyDraft` (`visit-drafts.ts`) only ever **created** (included + no pass) or **cancelled** (excluded + has pass). A name/phone edit on an already-issued guest fell through the gap → nothing happened → "No changes."

Fix: that third case (`include && hasActivePass`) now calls the existing **`updateVisitorInvite`**, which diffs the fields, updates the live invitation, and **resends the pass email** with the updated copy. The result flows through as `updated[]`, and the panel now shows **"N passes updated."** (engine: `visit-drafts.ts` + `routes.ts`; client: `types.ts` + `VisitPanel`). Engine build passes.

→ Needs your **local engine restarted** to pick it up.

## Bug 3 — "Manage Visitors" button flicker on refresh ✅ Improved + explained

Not background processing. It's the content script racing **Google Calendar's SPA hydration**: on a page refresh Google tears down and rebuilds the event surface several times, so our `readSurface()` momentarily reads "gone" and the button blinks. It settles once hydration finishes.

Fix: added a **400ms hide-debounce** — a transient "surface gone" tick no longer removes the button; it's only hidden if the surface stays gone. The trade-off is the button lingers ~400ms after you genuinely close an event, which is imperceptible.

## Bug 2 — nudge still without a name ❓ Need 2 data points

With 1A (no optimistic nudge unless it has a title) and 1B (complete sentence), a *truly* nameless nudge shouldn't be possible — sync targets always carry `ev.summary || '(no title)'`. So I want to see the actual data before changing code (rather than guess a third time). Two quick things:

1. **Exact nudge text** — which of these do you literally see?
   - `A visitor event needs passes.` (the 1B no-title fallback)
   - `Visitor event needs passes. (no title)`
   - `Visitor event needs passes.` with nothing after (would mean an old build)

2. **Dump the synced titles** — in the **background** console:
   ```js
   chrome.storage.local.get('auxilio.markedEvents').then(r =>
     console.table(Object.values(r['auxilio.markedEvents']||{})
       .map(m => ({ title: m.title, start: m.start, eventId: m.eventId }))));
   ```

If the titles are present there, the bug is in the nudge render/merge; if they're `(no title)` or blank, it's in capture (the event's `summary` wasn't synced) — and each points to a different, targeted fix. Paste both and I'll nail it.

Reload the extension to **0.11.0** for bugs 1 and 3.

> AGENT

Two clear improvements. Let me handle both.

**Bug A — nudge re-appears for already-sent events.** The *optimistic* DOM nudge (fired when you view then leave an event) bypasses the sync's handled-filter — so viewing a sent event re-adds it. The fix: check with the background whether the event is already handled before showing the optimistic nudge.

**Bug B — redesign** so the event name is the hero, not an afterthought.

58	
59	/** Drop the sync token so the next sync does a full tokenless re-scan of the
60	 *  forward window. Called on a real config change so it takes effect immediately
61	 *  instead of waiting for the periodic resync. */
62	export async function clearSyncToken(): Promise<void> {
63	  await chrome.storage.local.remove([SYNC_TOKEN_KEY, LAST_FULL_KEY]);
64	}
65	
66	/** Did the sync-relevant config (magic address or query schema) change since the
67	 *  last run? That's the ONLY time we must drop the token for a full re-scan. A
68	 *  plain reload leaves the signature unchanged, so it keeps the token and resumes
69	 *  cheap incremental sync — instead of re-running the multi-second full scan (which
70	 *  the MV3 worker can be killed in the middle of) on every reload. Records the new
71	 *  signature as a side effect. The 12h periodic full re-scan still self-heals drift. */
72	export async function syncConfigChanged(): Promise<boolean> {
73	  const sig = `${MAGIC_ADDRESS}|v${SYNC_SCHEMA_VERSION}`;
74	  const r = await chrome.storage.local.get(CONFIG_SIG_KEY);
75	  if (r[CONFIG_SIG_KEY] === sig) return false;
76	  await chrome.storage.local.set({ [CONFIG_SIG_KEY]: sig });
77	  return true;
78	}
79

  "new_string": "export async function readEngineHandled(): Promise<Set<string>> {\n  const r = await chrome.storage.local.get(ENGINE_HANDLED_KEY);\n  return new Set((r[ENGINE_HANDLED_KEY] as string[]) ?? []);\n}\n\n/** Has this event already been handled (passes issued) from THIS extension? Reads\n *  the local handled overlay — lets the in-page optimistic nudge skip an event the\n *  user already sent passes for, instead of re-nudging when they reopen/close it\n *  (the sync set already filters handled events, but the optimistic DOM guess\n *  bypasses that filter). engineHandled isn't consulted here: it's keyed by iCalUid,\n *  which the optimistic path doesn't have. */\nexport async function isEventHandled(eventId: string): Promise<boolean> {\n  if (!eventId) return false;\n  const handled = await readHandled();\n  return !!handled[eventId];\n}"
}

Both clean — built as **0.11.1**. Both changes done.

## Bug B — nudge redesign ✅

Restructured the banner so the **event name is the hero**, not a trailing clause:

```
🔔  Meeting 21                    [ Manage ]  ✕
    Needs visitor passes
```

- **Title row** — the event name, prominent (14.5px/600), ellipsised if long.
- **Subtitle** — "Needs visitor passes" (with `· +N more` when multiple events are pending).
- The bell icon top-aligns to the title; Manage/dismiss unchanged.

(The old layout was one run of text: *"**Visitor event needs passes.** Meeting 21"* — which is exactly why the name read as an afterthought.)

## Bug A — nudge re-appearing for already-sent events ✅

Root cause: the **optimistic DOM nudge** (fired when you view then leave a magic-address event) wrote straight to the banner, bypassing the sync's handled-filter. Since the sync never lists a handled event in `targets`, nothing ever dropped that optimistic entry, so it showed for its full 12s TTL whenever you reopened/closed a sent event.

Fix: before showing the optimistic nudge, the content script now asks the background **`IS_EVENT_HANDLED`** (checks the local handled overlay that `markHandled` writes on send). If the event's passes are already sent from this extension, no nudge.

One honest scope note: this checks the **local** handled overlay (this extension's sends), keyed by `eventId`. Passes sent via the **add-on or another device** are tracked by `engineHandled` keyed by `iCalUid`, which the optimistic path doesn't have — so an event you handled *elsewhere* could still briefly optimistic-nudge until the ~2s sync reconciles and filters it. Covering that fully would need the engine's iCalUid-based status, which the optimistic path lacks by design. Let me know if that edge matters and I'll wire it.

Reload to **0.11.1** to pick up both. (Bug 1's engine change from earlier still needs your local engine restarted.)

> AGENT

The resolver logic is intact, but notice line 175: the `.catch()` **silently swallows every error** — which is exactly why "not working" gives you no signal. The failure is almost certainly one of two things, and they need opposite fixes, so let me not guess. `people.ts` already logs failures to the **background** console, and we can test the People API directly there like we did for Calendar.

Two things to capture:

**1. Check the background console for existing warnings.** Open `chrome://extensions` → Auxilio → "Inspect views: service worker", reproduce (open an event with an unresolved guest), and look for:
```
[auxilio] searchContacts failed <email> Error: People people:searchContacts 403
[auxilio] otherContacts.search failed <email> …
```
A **403** = the access token lacks the contacts scopes (scope/consent problem). No warning at all = the API returned but found no match.

**2. Run this direct probe in the background console** — it separates "scope denied" from "contact genuinely not found":

```js
(async () => {
  const tok = (await chrome.storage.session.get('auxilio.tokens'))['auxilio.tokens'];
  if (!tok) { console.warn('not signed in'); return; }

  // (a) What scopes does the current access token actually carry?
  const info = await (await fetch(
    'https://www.googleapis.com/oauth2/v3/tokeninfo?access_token=' + tok.accessToken
  )).json();
  console.log('TOKEN SCOPES:', info.scope || info.error);

  // (b) Try a real contacts search for a guest email (replace with one you expect to resolve)
  const email = prompt('Guest email to test contacts lookup:');
  if (!email) return;
  const url = new URL('https://people.googleapis.com/v1/people:searchContacts');
  url.searchParams.set('query', email);
  url.searchParams.set('readMask', 'names,emailAddresses,photos');
  const res = await fetch(url, { headers: { Authorization: 'Bearer ' + tok.accessToken } });
  console.log('searchContacts status:', res.status);
  console.log('searchContacts body:', await res.text());
})();
```

What the result tells us:

| Result | Meaning | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| `TOKEN SCOPES` is **missing** `contacts.readonly` / `contacts.other.readonly` | You consented before those scopes were added; silent `prompt=none` renew can't widen scope | **Sign out + sign back in** to re-consent (then I can also force-trigger an interactive renew on scope-miss) |
| status **403** (`PERMISSION_DENIED` / People API not enabled) | Scope present but the People API isn't enabled on the OAuth project | Enable People API in the Google Cloud project |
| status **200** but `results` empty | The guest simply isn't in your Google Contacts / "other contacts" | Expected — People API only resolves people you've saved or emailed; not a bug |
| status **200** with a person but the name still doesn't show in the panel | Client-side merge issue | I fix `hooks.ts` |

My leading hypothesis is the **scope row** — you likely first signed in before the contacts scopes existed, and every renew since has been the silent `prompt=none` one (which I just reinforced with `login_hint`), so it never widened scope. If that's it, the proper fix is to **detect a contacts-scope 403 and trigger an interactive re-consent** instead of failing silently — but I want the token-scopes line to confirm before I build that.

Paste the `TOKEN SCOPES:` line and the `searchContacts status` and I'll fix the exact cause.

> AGENT

Scopes are present — both contacts scopes are there. So it's **not** a consent problem. The `prompt()` failed because service workers have no `prompt`. Here's a fixed probe — edit the `EMAIL` and `EXPECT` constants at the top. It replicates exactly what `people.ts` does (warmup first, then both searches):

```js
(async () => {
  const EMAIL = 'PUT_A_GUEST_EMAIL_HERE';   // an external guest you expect to resolve
  const tok = (await chrome.storage.session.get('auxilio.tokens'))['auxilio.tokens'];
  if (!tok) { console.warn('not signed in'); return; }
  const H = { Authorization: 'Bearer ' + tok.accessToken };

  const call = async (path, query, readMask) => {
    const url = new URL('https://people.googleapis.com/v1/' + path);
    url.searchParams.set('query', query);
    url.searchParams.set('readMask', readMask);
    url.searchParams.set('pageSize', '5');
    const res = await fetch(url, { headers: H });
    return { status: res.status, body: await res.text() };
  };

  // Warmup (People search index must be primed by a prior empty-query call)
  await call('people:searchContacts', '', 'names,emailAddresses').catch(()=>{});
  await call('otherContacts:search', '', 'names,emailAddresses').catch(()=>{});
  await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 800)); // let the index warm

  const c = await call('people:searchContacts', EMAIL, 'names,emailAddresses,photos');
  console.log('searchContacts →', c.status, c.body);

  const o = await call('otherContacts:search', EMAIL, 'names,emailAddresses');
  console.log('otherContacts  →', o.status, o.body);
})();
```

What I'm looking for:

- **status 403** on either → People API likely not enabled on the Cloud project (scopes alone don't enable the API).
- **status 200, empty `results`** → that guest isn't in your Contacts or "other contacts." The People API only returns people you've **saved or emailed before** — an external visitor you've never corresponded with simply won't resolve. That's a data reality, not a bug.
- **status 200 with a person + matching email** → the API works and the break is client-side (in `people.ts` `pick()` or the `hooks.ts` merge), which I'll then fix.

Also — important context so I target the right fix: is the guest you're testing **someone already in your Google Contacts**, or a brand-new external email you've never interacted with? If the latter, "not resolving" is expected behavior, and the real question becomes how we should display unresolved guests (keep the email-derived name) rather than a resolver bug.

Paste the two output lines and I'll pinpoint it.

> AGENT

That's the answer — and it clears the resolver of any API/scope/merge fault. The People API returns **"Junaid Ahmad"** perfectly for that email. So the lookup works; something between a working API and the panel is dropping it. The culprit is the **negative cache in `people.ts`**.

Here's the mechanism. `resolveOne` catches errors and returns `null`; `resolveGuests` caches that `null` in `storage.local` (`peopleCache`) for **7 days** as "looked up, not found." During your testing you had reloads and a scope/warmup window where an early lookup for this email **errored or raced the warmup** → returned `null` → got cached. Now, even though the API works, `resolveGuests` short-circuits on the cached `null` and never re-queries for a week.

Two real bugs there:
1. **A lookup *error* is cached identically to a genuine "not found."** A transient 403/network/warmup-race poisons resolution for 7 days.
2. No way to recover except waiting out the TTL.

Let me confirm with your cache, then fix it. Quick check in the background console:

```js
chrome.storage.local.get('auxilio.peopleCache').then(r =>
  console.log('cached entry:', JSON.stringify(r['auxilio.peopleCache']?.['j7654894110@gmail.com'])));
```

If it logs `{"person":null,"at":...}`, the negative-cache poisoning is confirmed.

Regardless of that output, the error-caching is unambiguously wrong, so let me fix it now. Reading the relevant part of `people.ts`:

98	/**
99	 * Resolve real name + photo for the given guest emails. Returns only the ones we
100	 * found something for. Cached (memory + storage.local with a TTL), looked up in
101	 * parallel, and best-effort — a failure for one email never rejects the whole call.
102	 */
103	export async function resolveGuests(
104	  accessToken: string,
105	  emails: string[],
106	): Promise<Record<string, ResolvedPerson>> {
107	  const want = [...new Set(emails.map((e) => e.toLowerCase()).filter(Boolean))];
108	  if (want.length === 0) return {};
109	
110	  const persisted = await loadCache();
111	  const out: Record<string, ResolvedPerson> = {};
112	  const todo: string[] = [];
113	
114	  for (const email of want) {
115	    const m = mem.get(email);
116	    if (m !== undefined) {
117	      if (m) out[email] = m;
118	      continue;
119	    }
120	    const p = persisted[email];
121	    if (p && Date.now() - p.at < CACHE_TTL_MS) {
122	      mem.set(email, p.person);
123	      if (p.person) out[email] = p.person;
124	      continue;
125	    }
126	    todo.push(email);
127	  }
128	
129	  if (todo.length > 0) {
130	    await warmup(accessToken);
131	    const results = await Promise.all(todo.map((e) => resolveOne(e, accessToken)));
132	    todo.forEach((email, i) => {
133	      const person = results[i];
134	      mem.set(email, person);
135	      persisted[email] = { person, at: Date.now() };
136	      if (person) out[email] = person;
137	    });
138	    await saveCache(persisted);
139	  }
140	  return out;
```

THROWS when a lookup errored and produced no\n *  hit — the result is indeterminate, so the caller must NOT cache it as not-found\n *  (a transient 403 / network blip / un-warmed index would otherwise poison the\n *  cache). This is the fix for \"contacts resolution silently stops working.\" */\nasync function resolveOne(email: string, accessToken: string): Promise<ResolvedPerson | null> {\n  let errored = false;\n  try {\n    const own = await search('people:searchContacts', email, 'names,emailAddresses,photos', accessToken);\n    for (const r of own.results ?? []) {\n      const hit = pick(r.person, email);\n      if (hit) return hit;\n    }\n  } catch (err) {\n    errored = true;\n    console.warn('[auxilio] searchContacts failed', email, err);\n  }\n  try {\n    // otherContacts readMask does not reliably support photos → names only.\n    const other = await search('otherContacts:search', email, 'names,emailAddresses', accessToken);\n    for (const r of other.results ?? []) {\n      const hit = pick(r.person, email);\n      if (hit) return hit;\n    }\n  } catch (err) {\n    errored = true;\n    console.warn('[auxilio] otherContacts.search failed', email, err);\n  }\n  if (errored) throw new Error(`people lookup indeterminate for ${email}`);\n  return null; // every lookup succeeded, no match → genuine not-found (cacheable)\n}"
}
```

CACHE_TTL_MS : NEG_CACHE_TTL_MS;\n    if (p && Date.now() - p.at < ttl) {\n      mem.set(email, p.person);\n      if (p.person) out[email] = p.person;\n      continue;\n    }\n    todo.push(email);\n  }\n\n  if (todo.length > 0) {\n    await warmup(accessToken);\n    const results = await Promise.allSettled(todo.map((e) => resolveOne(e, accessToken)));\n    let dirty = false;\n    todo.forEach((email, i) => {\n      const r = results[i];\n      // Transient failure (rejected) → do NOT cache; leave it to retry next open.\n      // Only persist a definitive answer (a person, or a real not-found null).\n      if (r.status !== 'fulfilled') return;\n      const person = r.value;\n      mem.set(email, person);\n      persisted[email] = { person, at: Date.now() };\n      if (person) out[email] = person;\n      dirty = true;\n    });\n    if (dirty) await saveCache(persisted);\n  }\n  return out;"

  "new_string": "async function loadCache(): Promise<Record<string, CacheEntry>> {\n  try {\n    const r = await chrome.storage.local.get([CACHE_KEY, CACHE_VERSION_KEY]);\n    // Drop a cache written by an older (buggy) version — e.g. the one that cached\n    // lookup errors as a 7-day \"not found\". One-time, on the first load after a bump.\n    if (r[CACHE_VERSION_KEY] !== CACHE_VERSION) {\n      await chrome.storage.local.set({ [CACHE_VERSION_KEY]: CACHE_VERSION, [CACHE_KEY]: {} });\n      return {};\n    }\n    return (r[CACHE_KEY] as Record<string, CacheEntry>) ?? {};\n  } catch {\n    return {};\n  }"
}

> AGENT

Clean — built as **0.11.2**.

## Root cause (confirmed, not guessed)

Your probe proved the People API returns **"Junaid Ahmad"** for that email — scopes, consent, API, and `pick()` all work. The break was the **negative cache in `people.ts`**:

`resolveOne` caught lookup errors and returned `null`, which `resolveGuests` then cached in `storage.local` for **7 days** as "not found" — identical to a genuine miss. During your earlier reloads / scope transition / warmup races, an early lookup for this email errored, cached `null`, and every load since short-circuited on that poisoned entry. The API recovering didn't matter; the cache never re-queried.

## Fix (0.11.2)

1. **Errors are no longer cached.** `resolveOne` now **throws** when a lookup errors with no hit (indeterminate); `resolveGuests` uses `allSettled` and **skips caching** rejected lookups, so they retry next open. Only a definitive answer (a person, or a real no-match) is cached.
2. **Negative results expire fast** — a genuine "not found" now lives **6 hours** instead of 7 days, so a guest you later save to Contacts resolves the same day. Found names still cache 7 days.
3. **One-time drop of the poisoned cache** — a `CACHE_VERSION` bump clears the existing bad `peopleCache` on first load, so your stuck `null` for that email is wiped immediately.

## To verify

Reload to **0.11.2**, open the event with that guest → it should now show **"Junaid Ahmad"** (the version bump clears the poisoned entry, and the live lookup succeeds). If you want to watch it: the background console will no longer have a stale cache hit, and the lookup returns the name.

That `peopleCache` check I asked for is now moot (the cache is auto-cleared on this load), but if you ran it before reloading and it showed `{"person":null,...}`, that's the poisoning confirmed.

One honest note: this fixes resolution going forward and unsticks the poisoned entries. The deeper "names must reach the sent pass/email" path is the 0.10.3 persistence change — that only fires once a name actually resolves, so with the resolver unstuck, persistence will now work too. Worth re-testing a fresh send to confirm the pass/email carry the real name.

So the one thing I need to know: **after reloading to 0.11.2, does the guest now display as "Junaid Ahmad" in the panel, or still the email-derived fallback?**

That answer splits cleanly:

- **It shows "Junaid Ahmad" now** → fixed. The negative-cache poisoning was the bug; the version bump unstuck it. Done. (And re-test a fresh send to confirm the pass/email carry the real name via the 0.10.3 persistence.)

- **Cache has the name but the panel still shows the fallback** → the resolver is healthy but a **downstream merge/display** step isn't applying it, which is a different bug. The most likely cause then is that the engine persisted the *fallback* name with `nameIsFallback: false` at some point, so `useResolveGuestNames` skips the guest (it only runs for `nameIsFallback === true`) — and the resolved cache value never gets merged in.

Built **0.11.3** with diagnostics. The cache is healthy, so the break is in the panel's resolve/merge path — these two logs will show exactly where.

## Please do this

1. Reload the extension to **0.11.3**.
2. Open the **side panel** console: right-click inside the open side panel → **Inspect** (this is the panel's own console, *not* the background one).
3. Reproduce: create/open the event, open the panel so the guests load.
4. Paste the two `[auxilio] resolve-names:` lines:
   - `roster [...] | pending [...]`
   - `RESOLVE_GUESTS returned {...}`

## What each outcome tells us (and the fix it points to)

| What you see | Meaning | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| `pending []` is **empty**, but a guest's `fb` (nameIsFallback) is **false** while its `name` is the email-ish fallback | The engine stored the fallback name as authoritative (`nameIsFallback:false`), so the resolver never runs for it | Re-derive `nameIsFallback` on load / re-resolve names that still look email-derived |
| `pending` has the email, but **no** `RESOLVE_GUESTS returned` line appears | The effect isn't firing (a deps/timing issue) | Fix the effect wiring |
| `RESOLVE_GUESTS returned {}` (empty) despite the cache having the name | The background lookup returns nothing — likely the People warmup race (index not primed on a cold worker) | Add a warmup settle/retry in `people.ts` |
| `RESOLVE_GUESTS returned {j7654...: {name:"Junaid Ahmad"}}` but the panel still shows the fallback | The merge isn't reaching the row — a reconciliation issue in `RosterRow` | Fix the merge/display |

My current lead is the **first row** — that the guest came back with `nameIsFallback: false` and an email-derived name, so the resolver skips it. But the `fb` value in the log will confirm it outright instead of me guessing. Paste those lines and I'll fix the exact step.
